# Decisions log
Append-only. One entry per choice an agent made without asking (per ask-rule).

## 2026-09-18 — /health served without trailing slash only (B-000)
- chose: route `path('health', ...)` so exactly `GET /health` returns 200; `/health/` is not routed (404).
- because: acceptance names `/health` literally; monitors and load-balancer probes hit a fixed string, so one canonical URL is simpler. Redirects (301 via APPEND_SLASH) are not useful for probes, and a trailing-slash-only route would break the acceptance path.
- considered: (a) `path('health/')` relying on APPEND_SLASH to redirect `/health` -> `/health/` (adds a 301, fails "exactly /health"); (b) registering both routes (duplicate names, no real gain).
- reversible: yes (add a second `path('health/')` line if ever needed).

## 2026-09-18 — Index view no longer requires login (B-101)
- chose: drop `@login_required` from `spelling_game.views.index`; `GET /` renders the game for anonymous users. Added a test for it.
- because: CLAUDE.md says "Never: login required to play" and "Free to play without login"; with the decorator a fresh load of `/` is a 302 to the login page, so B-101's `theme.png` (fresh load) could not exist. The health endpoint had already been made auth-free for the same reason.
- considered: (a) teaching `tools/shot.mjs` to log in first (needs credentials, a cookie flow, and keeps a rule violation alive); (b) shooting the login page as `theme.png` (fails the acceptance).
- reversible: yes (one decorator line), but it should not be reverted per product rules.

## 2026-09-18 — How tools/shot.mjs finds and drives Chromium (B-101)
- chose: resolve the newest `~/.cache/ms-playwright/chromium-<rev>/chrome-linux/chrome` (numeric sort on `<rev>`), falling back to the newest `chromium_headless_shell-<rev>/chrome-linux/headless_shell`; `CHROMIUM=/path` env overrides. Launch with `--headless=new --remote-debugging-port=0` in a temp `--user-data-dir`, parse the `DevTools listening on ws://…` line from stderr, and talk CDP over Node's built-in `WebSocket` (Target/Page/Runtime/Emulation/Network). Every step (launch, connect, navigate, load, eval, capture) has a 15s timeout; the browser is SIGKILLed and the temp profile removed on every exit path.
- because: the backlog names only "the headless Chromium under `~/.cache/ms-playwright/`"; not hardcoding the revision keeps the tool working after a Playwright update, and the full `chromium-*` build renders fonts like a real phone browser, which the headless shell does not always match. The port approach needs no fd plumbing and works with the global `WebSocket` in Node 22 (no packages).
- also: a main-document HTTP status >= 400 is treated as a navigation failure (exit 1), and page-side uncaught exceptions are printed as `shot: page error:` warnings without failing the shot, so reviewers see broken JS in the log.
- considered: `--remote-debugging-pipe` (fd 3/4, more code); Playwright's own node module (not installed, and CLAUDE.md forbids new dependencies).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-18 — Tracked copies under staticfiles/ left in place (B-102)
- chose: delete only `static/js/script.js`, `static/js/ui.js`, `static/css/style.css`, `static/css/mobile-fixes.css` and the unimported `static/js/data.js`. The collected copies under `staticfiles/{css,js}/` stay.
- because: `staticfiles/` is `STATIC_ROOT` (collectstatic output) and is listed in `.gitignore`, yet its files are tracked from an earlier commit. The acceptance names `templates static` only; untracking `staticfiles/` wholesale touches every admin asset and is unrelated to the frontend rewrite. In DEBUG, runserver serves from `static/` via the finders, so the stale copies are not what the browser loads.
- considered: `git rm --cached -r staticfiles` (large unrelated diff); deleting just the five collected copies (leaves the directory half-tracked and inconsistent).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-18 — Acceptance grep hits inside base64 image data treated as false positives (B-102)
- chose: treat `grep -rn "script.js\|ui.js\|style.css\|mobile-fixes" templates static` as satisfied although it prints two lines: `static/images/animals/horse.svg:45` (`uiIjs`) and `static/images/transportation/bicycle.svg:45` (`uiwjs`). Both are inside base64 `data:image/jpeg` blobs; the unescaped `.` in `ui.js` matches any byte. The same grep with `ui\.js` (dot escaped), or with `--exclude-dir=images`, prints nothing.
- because: the matches are not references to the deleted files, and content assets under `static/images/` are read-only in M1 (CLAUDE.md), so the only way to make the literal grep empty would break that rule.
- considered: editing the two SVGs (forbidden); asking the human (reversible, no user-facing effect, so the ask-rule says decide and log).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-19 — Themes live in state; theme screen renders from it (B-103)
- chose: add `state.themes` (`[{ name, dataUrl, image }]`). `main.js` loads `static/config/themes.json` and every theme's data file once at start-up, takes the first word's `image`, and calls `setState({ themes })`; `renderThemeButtons(state)` is a subscriber that rebuilds the cards only when the array is replaced. `startRound` receives the theme entry and re-fetches its data file (browser cache hit).
- because: CLAUDE.md says every screen is a render of state and state lives only in `state.js`; the previous code built the buttons once outside of state. The acceptance needs the first word's image, which is only in the per-theme data file, so those files must be read before the theme screen can render.
- considered: fetching the images lazily per button (flash of image-less cards); keeping the buttons outside state (violates the architecture rule).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-19 — Palette applied to the play and round-end screens too (B-103)
- chose: to leave no colour literal outside `:root`, elements the theme item does not name were mapped as well: tiles and letter boxes are white surfaces with the DESIGN shadow (empty boxes keep a dashed frame in the muted colour); Check/Next/Play again and the logout pill are primary teal with the text colour as label (white on #7FB7BE is ~2.3:1 contrast); live hearts are the accent colour and lost hearts muted (B-106 owns the error-colour transition); the dragged-tile shadow is the DESIGN shadow colour at 0.24 alpha (`--shadow-lift`). Body text is the system sans-serif at 16px; Fredoka One is used for headings, tiles and buttons per DESIGN "Type".
- because: the acceptance says no other colour literals remain in `game.css`; B-104/B-106 refine these screens later and can change the mapping.
- considered: leaving old literals in place on non-theme screens (fails the acceptance); white button labels (contrast too low for a beginner reader).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-19 — tools/shot.mjs gets `--viewport WxH` (B-103)
- chose: an optional `--viewport <W>x<H>` (CSS px, default `360x740`) that only changes width and height; device scale stays 2 and mobile emulation stays on, so `theme-desktop.png` is 2048x1480. Bad values exit 1 with a usage line (test added). Usage comment stays five lines.
- because: the acceptance needs a 1024x740 screenshot and the task said to extend the tool minimally rather than add another one.
- considered: switching `mobile` off above a width threshold (extra heuristic nobody asked for).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-20 — Letter boxes wrap into even rows instead of shrinking below 48px (B-104)
- chose: boxes are 48px like the tiles. When a word does not fit one row, boxes of 8+ letters shrink toward 40px only if that makes the row fit; otherwise they stay 48px and wrap into rows as even as possible (7 letters at 360px -> 4 + 3, 9 -> 5 + 4, 10 -> 5 + 5). The rule lives in `static/js/layout.js` (`boxLayout(count, width, gap)`, pure, node-tested); `card.js` applies it as `--box-size` / `--cols` on `#letter-boxes` (CSS grid), and `main.js` re-renders the card on `resize`.
- because: at 360px wide with 16px gutters the row is 328px. Seven 48px boxes (372px) and even eight 40px boxes (362px with 6px gaps) do not fit one row, so the DESIGN minimums (48px; 40px only for 8+ letters) force a second row for the 7-, 9- and 10-letter words that already exist in the content. Wrapping keeps every box a full tap target; even rows read better than flex-wrap's 6 + 1. On the 480px desktop column the shrink still applies (9 letters -> 44px, one row).
- considered: shrinking boxes to whatever fits one row (the previous CSS: 27px boxes for "helicopter", fails the 40px floor); letting the box row bleed into the gutters (only rescues 8-letter words, and there are none in the content); hearts and counter on one line to buy height (the item lists them as separate rows).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-20 — Play screen: fixed card, buttons pinned above a 96px mascot strip, primary tile inside a filled box (B-104)
- chose: `#screen-play` is top-aligned with 8px gaps, a `min(48vw, 192px)` square card, and `padding-bottom: 96px` (`--mascot-corner`); the action row uses `margin-top: auto` so Check/Next sit just above that strip in the same place for every word length. A filled box is primary-coloured and the tile inside it also takes the primary colour (no shadow); a tile dragged out keeps the colour until it lands. Empty boxes are white with the DESIGN shadow and a dashed muted frame. Disabled Check is 0.4 opacity with no shadow.
- because: with hearts, counter, two rows of boxes and two rows of tiles for a 10-letter word, the column needs about 640px above the mascot strip at 740px tall, which fixes the card at roughly 176px on a 360px phone. Reserving the whole bottom strip (not just the corner) is the simplest way to keep the bottom-left 96x96 free with full-width buttons, and a constant button position is easier for a kid's thumb. The tile covers the box, so "filled boxes use the primary colour" is only visible if the tile itself is teal.
- considered: buttons right-aligned beside the corner (110px buttons, asymmetric); card that grows into free space (`flex: 1` + `aspect-ratio`, fragile across browsers); keeping the placed tile white on a teal box (teal never visible).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-20 — Tile motion runs after setState, from the old position to the rendered one (B-105)
- chose: a tap or drop first goes through `setState` (card.js moves the tile element into its box or back to the tray, transform reset), then `drag.js` measures where the tile was and where the render put it and tweens the difference to zero (FLIP). Tap-to-place uses the same drop snap as a drag (`back.out(1.4)`, 200ms) and so does tapping a placed tile back to the tray; a drop that hits neither a box nor the tray slides back with `power2.out` in 250ms. The numbers live in `static/js/motion.js` (pure, node-tested); `motionFor(kind, reduced)` returns duration 0, no scale, no overshoot when `prefers-reduced-motion` matches, which drag.js reads via `matchMedia` at each gesture.
- because: CLAUDE.md says state is the only truth and every screen is a render of it, so the tile cannot animate "into" a box before the state says it is there; animating after the render keeps the motion purely visual and one code path serves drag and tap. DESIGN "Motion" allows the overshoot only for the drop into a box, so the stray-drop return (previously `back.out(1.4)` 300ms) is now a plain ease-out inside the 300ms cap.
- considered: animating to the box first and committing state on tween end (state lags the finger, a second tap mid-tween would read stale state); a CSS transition on the tile (cannot move between parents); keeping the overshoot on the stray return (contradicts DESIGN).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-20 — Reduced motion keeps the lifted shadow, instantly (B-105)
- chose: under `prefers-reduced-motion` the picked-up tile still shows the lifted shadow (`.gsap-dragging`), but with no CSS transition and no scale; snaps and returns are `gsap.set`, not tweens.
- because: the acceptance says "no scale or overshoot, snaps are instant"; the shadow is a static state that tells the kid the tile is held, not motion, and DESIGN "reduce to opacity fades" is about animation.
- considered: dropping the shadow too (loses the only held-state cue for reduced-motion users).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-22 — Rule suggestions from the B-106 review applied (issue #13)
- chose: the human answered issue #13 "A apply, B apply". Applied verbatim: (A) DESIGN.md "Motion" now says the lost heart turns to the error colour at once and fades to muted over 200ms when the kid next moves a tile (or on Next); (B) BACKLOG.md B-109 acceptance says the lost heart (already error-coloured from B-106) fades to muted over 200ms when the kid next moves a tile, alongside the wrong tint clearing. Issue #11 (B-105 review) was consumed by the previous run and is logged on the B-106 branch (PR #12), so it is not repeated here.
- because: question protocol; answers are consumed at the start of the next run and logged here.
- reversible: yes (doc lines).

## 2026-09-22 — Round-end layout, star and mark rendering, Play again via startRound (B-107)
- chose: `#screen-round-end` mirrors the play screen: top-aligned column, `padding-bottom: 96px` (`--mascot-corner`) and the action row pushed down with `margin-top: auto`, so Play again / Themes sit just above the free bottom-left strip. Stars are three 64px inline SVGs in the accent colour, outline only (stroke) until earned, then filled (class `earned`, toggled by `renderRoundEnd`). Each word is a 48px white strip with a 40px thumbnail, the word in Fredoka One and a tick (accent) or cross (error colour) drawn as inline SVG; thumbnails come from `state.words[i].image` since `round.results[i]` holds only `{ word, correct }`. "Play again" looks up the current theme entry in `state.themes` and calls the existing `startRound`, which re-fetches the data file (browser cache), picks new random words and resets hearts and results; a new `#themes-btn` "Themes" handler resets theme, words, round and current and shows the theme screen (hearts are reset by `startRound`, so the handler does not touch `lives`). `starsFor(correct, size)` is written against `size` (all right -> 3, one short -> 2, at least 40% -> 1, else 0; the 5-word bands from the backlog) and throws on a non-integer or out-of-range score.
- because: DESIGN reserves the bottom-left corner on the round-end screen and B-104 already established the strip approach for full-width buttons; the same shape keeps both screens consistent. Inline SVG (as with the hearts) is the only way the accent colour is guaranteed on Android, where glyphs fall back to the colour-emoji font. Reusing `startRound` keeps one code path for "new round in theme X" and honours the state-only architecture. Tick in accent and cross in error match the palette roles ("correct", "lost heart"). The B-106 branch replaces `LIVES` with `HEARTS` in this file, so the new handlers avoid that constant to keep the merge small.
- considered: caching the theme's data in state to avoid the re-fetch (more state for no visible gain); a star glyph (colour-emoji fallback problem); a muted outline for unearned stars (accent outline reads as "three to collect", which is the reward the kickoff asked for).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-26 — Correct feedback and star pop as a transition-keyed subscriber; stars get a fill layer (B-110)
- chose: a new `static/js/celebrate.js` subscriber (`renderCelebrate`, wired in `main.js` after `renderRoundEnd`) keeps a render cache `last = { screen, status }` and fires only on transitions: status became 'correct' -> the boxes pulse and confetti bursts; screen became 'round-end' -> the earned stars pop. A re-render in the same state (every setState while the screen shows) does nothing; leaving the state kills the tweens and clears the inline transform/opacity so the CSS render of state shows again. Named `celebrate.js`, not `feedback.js`, because the open B-109 branch adds a `feedback.js` (wiggle) and an add/add conflict is the one merge hunk that cannot be resolved trivially; the two modules can be folded into one after both land.
- numbers in `motion.js` (node-tested; `CelebrateMotionSpecTests` is its own class at the end of tests.py so B-109's hunks inside `MotionSpecTests` do not overlap): `pulse` scale 1.1 over 300ms (150 up, 150 back, `power2.out` mirrored, no overshoot per DESIGN); `confetti` 40 pieces, 600ms `power2.out`, started together with the pulse so the whole beat is 600ms; `starPop` scale 0 -> 1 with `back.out(1.4)` in 200ms, stagger 200ms (each star starts when the previous lands). Reduced-motion contract extended: `duration: 0, ease: 'none', scale: 1` as before; a kind with `count` gets 0 (no confetti); a kind with `fade` keeps it (0.2s) and the consumer does an opacity-only fade instead (boxes 0.4 -> 1, star fills 0 -> 1 one after another).
- confetti: 40 `span.confetti-piece` (8x12px, radius 2) cycling the accent, primary and error palette roles via CSS classes (no colour literals), inside a `div.confetti` layer appended to `<body>`: `position: fixed; inset: 0; overflow: hidden; pointer-events: none`, so input is never blocked and a piece past the edge never widens the page. Pieces start inside the middle half of the card (`getBoundingClientRect`) and fly outward past its edge on evenly spread, jittered angles with a small downward drift, spinning; opacity fades over the last 40%. The GSAP timeline's `onComplete` removes the layer.
- stars: each star span in the template now holds two SVGs, the outline and a `fill` layer (`position: absolute; inset: 0; opacity: 0`, `opacity: 1` under `.earned`); the pop scales only the fill, so the accent outline stays visible under a star that has not popped yet (DESIGN: unearned stars are accent outlines). The old `.stars .earned svg { fill }` rule is replaced by `.stars .earned .fill`; at rest the screen looks exactly as in B-107's screenshots.
- considered: triggering from `onCheck` / `onNext` (conflicts with B-106 and ties an effect to one caller); popping the whole star span (the outline would vanish for up to 600ms, leaving a gap); confetti appended inside `.card` (needs position/overflow changes on the card and sits under later siblings); a CSS keyframe pulse on the boxes (replays on every render while correct).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-26 — Sticker book API and where a sticker is added (B-111)
- chose: `static/js/stickers.js` has no module state and no DOM: every call reads `globalThis.localStorage` (key `flip.stickers.v1`), so the module runs unchanged under node with a fake `localStorage` and the book is never a second copy of the truth. `load()` returns the sanitised book object `{ "<theme>": ["duck", ...] }`: bad JSON, a non-object, a theme whose value is not an array, non-string or empty entries and duplicates are all dropped on read, and a missing `localStorage` (or one that throws) reads as `{}`. `all()` returns the same book flattened to `[{ theme, word }]` in book order (B-112 needs totals for the empty state and per-theme counts; the two shapes cover both). `add(theme, word)` returns `true` when the word was new and `false` when the book already had it, writing only in the first case; a failed write (quota, private mode) is swallowed so the round still plays. The sticker is added in `onCheck` inside the existing `if (correct)` branch, which is exactly "checked correct with at least one heart left" (a third miss goes down the reveal branch and never reaches it), and the return value is recorded in state as `round.results[i].newSticker`; the round-end render counts and badges from that field, not from the book, so "new" means new at the moment the word was mastered and survives re-renders and a later `add` of the same word. Nothing else in the game reads the book yet.
- because: CLAUDE.md wants state as the only truth and renders that read state, not the DOM or a side store; recording `newSticker` at check time is the one place the answer exists (by round-end every mastered word is already in the book, so a render-time `has()` would always say "not new"). Reading storage on every call costs nothing at this size and keeps the module free of a cache that would count as game state in a module global. The `onCheck` hunk is one comment and one line inside the branch B-106 (PR #12) keeps, so the merge overlap stays small.
- considered: a subscriber that adds the sticker on the status transition to 'correct' (cannot record "new" into state without a nested setState during a render); snapshotting the book at round start into state and diffing at round end (more state, same result); `all()` returning the book object again (redundant with `load()`).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-26 — "New stickers: N" line and the sticker badge on the round-end screen (B-111)
- chose: a `<p id="round-new">` under the score, body font at 1.1rem (17.6px) in the muted colour like the word counter, always shown including "New stickers: 0" (the acceptance names a `round-end-no-new` state, and a line that disappears would read as a bug to a kid who got one last time). The badge is a 28px accent-coloured circle with a white (surface) star, placed between the word and the tick on rows whose result has `newSticker`; it is decorative (`aria-label="new sticker"`), so the 48px tap-target rule does not apply. Height budget at 360x740 is now 48+8+64+12+40+12+22+12+272+12+56+96 = 654, still no scrolling; measured: the Themes button ends at 644px, the 96px strip stays free. CSS cache-buster bumped `?v=7` -> `?v=8` as previous items did when game.css changed.
- because: DESIGN "Tone of text" wants very little, plain English; a count is the shortest honest line. The star on a round sticker echoes the round's stars (accent = reward) without a new colour or glyph font, and a small icon reads faster than a "NEW" label for a beginner reader.
- considered: hiding the line at 0 (see above); a "NEW" text label (more text, English-only readers); a badge on the thumbnail corner (overlaps the image and is harder to see at 40px).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-27 — Sticker book screen: word lists in state, book read at render time (B-112)
- chose: `loadThemes` in `main.js` already fetches every theme's data file at start-up (B-103), so each entry in `state.themes` now also keeps `words: [{ word, image }]` in file order; nothing else is fetched when the gallery opens. A new `static/js/gallery.js` subscriber (`renderGallery`, wired after `renderRoundEnd`) renders `#screen-gallery` when `state.screen === 'gallery'`: it calls `stickers.load()` once per render and rebuilds one `<section>` per theme (heading with the count, a `<ul>` grid) from `state.themes` and that book; the book is not copied into state. `themeProgress(book, theme, words)` is the pure part (node-tested, `GalleryProgressTests`): it returns `{ count, total, items }` where only words in the theme's list count, so a sticker for a word that left the content or a malformed theme entry can never show "15 / 14". "Stickers" (`#stickers-btn`, theme screen) does `setState({ screen: 'gallery' })` and "Back" (`#gallery-back-btn`) does `setState({ screen: 'theme' })`; neither touches any other state. New ids added to the template: `stickers-btn`, `gallery-back-btn`, `gallery-empty`, `gallery-themes`.
- because: CLAUDE.md wants every screen to be a render of state and the book read only through `stickers.js`; B-111 made the book itself the truth for "already mastered" with no second copy, and copying it into state on open would create one. The word lists have to come from the read-only data files, which are already in memory at start-up; keeping them on the theme entry avoids a second fetch path and an async render. Rebuilding the sections on every render is fine: nothing in them is interactive and no setState fires while the gallery shows except Back.
- considered: fetching the data file on open and storing the words in state (async render, more state for no visible gain); a `book` field in state refreshed on open (second copy of the truth); a DOM cache to skip the rebuild (nothing to preserve).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-09-27 — Sticker book layout and copy (B-112)
- chose: the "Stickers" button sits under the theme cards inside the existing `.actions` row with class `action-btn`, so it is the same 56px primary button as Check/Next and adds no CSS; at 360x740 the theme screen still fits without scrolling (measured 740px). On the gallery, Back is a 48px-tall primary pill on the left and the "Stickers" h2 is centred in the column (grid `1fr auto 1fr`); the screen is top-aligned with no mascot strip and the page scrolls (1441px tall with both themes). Each theme is an h3 (Fredoka One 1.3rem) with the name left and the count right in the muted colour; below it a 3-column grid (`repeat(3, minmax(0, 1fr))`, 8px gaps -> 104px cells at 360px, 144px on the 480px column; three columns on desktop too). A cell is a white paper tile with the square image and the word under it in Fredoka One 1rem ("helicopter" measures 79px in the 96px inner width, one line); unmastered cells use `filter: grayscale(1) opacity(0.45)` on the image and a muted "?" for the word. The empty state is one muted body line above the grids, "No stickers yet. Go spell some!" (one line at 360px, the screen's only exclamation mark); the greyed grids with "0 / 14" still show underneath so the kid sees what there is to collect. CSS cache-buster `?v=8` -> `?v=9`. Screenshots: `gallery-empty.png`, `gallery-some.png` (seeded duck, cat, frog, bus), plus `theme.png` (the button is a change to that screen) and `gallery-some-bottom.png` (the same seeded state scrolled to the end, to show the second theme and that nothing is clipped).
- because: DESIGN "Tone of text" wants very little plain English; a count and a "?" need no reading. Greying via `filter` is what the acceptance names and keeps the picture recognisable as a hint. `minmax(0, 1fr)` is what keeps a long word from widening a column (the "nothing clipped horizontally" line). Showing the greyed grids in the empty state follows the acceptance literally ("for each theme ... a grid") and makes the empty line read as an invitation, not an error.
- considered: hiding the grids in the empty state (the screen would be a line of text); a two-column grid on phones (bigger images but a taller page for 26 words); a "NEW" tag or count on the Stickers button (not asked, more text on the theme screen); a desktop shot (DESIGN asks for one only when the item names it).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-04 — Play screen as a lab scene column; `data-screen` CSS hook on <body> (B-113)
- chose: `#screen-play` is one column `--scene-w` wide (full width on a phone, at most 480px) with `bg-lab.jpg` as its background (`100% auto`, anchored top, `--bench-deep` below). Everything drawn onto the picture is placed in % of the column width and of the picture height (`--bg-h` = width x 2739 / 1264): the TV picture at x 28-70% / y 16.4-30.5%, the brass plate at x 24.5-72.5% / y 33.3-38.5%; their contents are sized in `--u` (1px at 360 wide) so they scale with width. The rail starts at 44.5% of the picture height (y 347 at 360 wide, "directly below the machine"), the bench of blocks follows, and the interim controls sit at the bottom of the safe area (`margin-top: auto`); left/right/bottom padding is the frame's safe area (14px, 14px, 22px). The frame is a fixed, click-through `div.frame` at the end of `<body>` with the border-image exactly as DESIGN.md (`z-index: 20`, above the confetti layer); `#screen-play` gets `isolation: isolate` so a dragged block's Draggable z-index boost stays under the frame. `screens.js` writes `document.body.dataset.screen = state.screen` on every render; CSS uses it to show the frame, darken the page around the column and lift the header over the shelf on the play screen only. Above 480px wide (desktop) the column is `min(480px, 100vh x 360/740)` so the phone composition fits the window instead of scrolling.
- because: DESIGN.md "Layout" defines the TV and plate in % of the background image, so they must share the image's coordinate system; the header and the frame live outside `#screen-play` and need to know which screen shows. The attribute is render output like `style.display` and the `filled` / `lost` classes: written from state, never read by a script, so it is not game state on the DOM. The frame and the lab background stay off the other screens because their interim look is B-116.
- considered: a body class per screen (same thing, less general for B-116); moving the logo into `#screen-play` (duplicates the header); `:has()` selectors on the hidden screens (fragile); a 480px desktop column without the height cap (a 10-letter word needs ~990px at 480 wide and would scroll).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-04 — v5 palette, tints in :root, and a plain mapping for the interim screens (B-113)
- chose: `:root` defines the 13 DESIGN.md colours under their DESIGN names (`--bench` ... `--error`) and removes every old `--color-*` variable. Highlights and shaded edges are declared in `:root` only, as `color-mix(in srgb, var(--palette) 85%, #fff|#000)` (a tint or shade of at most 15%); shadows, glows and scanlines are palette colours made translucent (`color-mix(..., transparent)`). No colour literal or `color-mix` appears outside `:root`; the palette test now checks the v5 names and values, that every mix is a palette colour with at most 15% white or black (or transparent), that no old variable and no named colour remains, and that the frame's border-image is the DESIGN string. Theme, round-end and sticker book are not restyled: their old roles are mapped mechanically (page paper -> `--cream`, white surfaces -> `--cream-hi`, primary buttons and theme cards -> `--brass` with `--ink` labels, accent stars / ticks / sticker badge -> `--amber-deep`, muted text -> `--brass-dark`, cross -> `--error`); the confetti pieces become amber, teal glow and cream (DESIGN "Feedback" sparks) without renaming celebrate.js's classes.
- because: the acceptance says the DESIGN palette replaces the old variables with no other colour literals, and DESIGN allows tints within ±15%; mixing with at most 15% white or black is the strictest reading and is checkable. The interim screens must keep working and stay readable until B-116; brass with ink labels is about 5.5:1 contrast.
- noted: the v5 mockup's block faces (about #fbf8e8) are lighter than `--cream` plus 15% white (#f1e5c8), so the blocks read slightly warmer than the reference.
- considered: hex literals for the tints (harder to verify against the 15% rule); leaving the old variables for the interim screens (fails "replace the old ones").
- reversible: yes.

- chose: the word picture is shown whole (`object-fit: contain`, square, 6px inset from the tube, 8px corners) on the teal tube (`--teal` to a 15% shade at the edges), masked to the screen's rounded corners, with 1px scanlines every 3px (ink at 16%) over picture and tube and a soft teal-glow inset and halo. Lives are three 15px (x `--u`) bulbs on the plate, lit `--amber` (radial highlight, `--amber-deep` rim, amber glow), lost `--bulb-off`; they reuse the `.hearts span` / `.lost` hooks so card.js and the open B-106/B-109 changes still find them. The counter is one Courier Prime Bold flap per character ("2", "/", "5"; 22x29 and 16x29 at 360 wide), cream on a charcoal flap with a hinge line; the "flip" logo is four 20x26 flaps, the last letter in `--amber-deep` as in the mockup, at (36, 36) on the play screen. On the play screen the header's auth line ("Hi, name!" / "Logout") is hidden because it is English; it stays on the theme screen. The mute toggle's place top-right is left empty (B-108, PR #16, is not merged). The picture's `alt` is now empty (English if the image failed).

The mockup's flap and bulb sizes were measured from `play-screen-v5.png` (a 3x render of 360x740).
- considered: `cover` (crops the subject); blending the picture into the tube with `mix-blend-mode` (tints the subject teal); keeping the SVG hearts (DESIGN: bulbs).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-04 — Slot rail and letter blocks: fixed spots, rows of four, tilt through GSAP (B-113)
- chose: `layout.js` replaces `boxLayout` with two pure, node-tested helpers. `slotLayout(count, railWidth)`: 46px slots, 6px gap, 8px rail padding, at most six to a row even when a wider rail would fit seven (DESIGN: "Six slots fit one row; 7+ letters wrap into two even rows"), fewer only if the rail is too narrow; the B-104 shrink-to-40px rule is gone. `blockLayout(count, width)`: 52px blocks (the DESIGN minimum; the mockup measures about 55), rows filled four at a time with the remainder centred on the last row (6 -> 4 + 2 as in the mockup, 10 -> 4 + 4 + 2), 16px between blocks and 12px between rows, rows shifted alternately 8px left and right when there are two or more, plus a fixed per-position tilt (-3.5 to 3.5 degrees) and a 0-3px nudge from small tables, so the layout is the same on every render. card.js builds one absolutely placed `.block-spot` per tray tile in `#letter-tray` (a DOM cache, rebuilt when the tile count changes, repositioned every render) and mounts each unused block into its own spot; a used block leaves its spot empty, so the others never shuffle and a returned block lands where it started (the "start spots" B-114's reset lever needs). Blocks are tilted through GSAP (`rotation`, since GSAP owns the transform) and are upright in a slot, where they fill the 46px hole with a 28px letter (32px on the bench). drag.js's FLIP move now also eases the tilt from the old place to the new one instead of snapping it; nothing else in drag.js changed. The rail is brass (highlight, body, darker lip) with dark sunk holes; blocks are cream clay with a shaded lower-right edge and a contact shadow, lifted further while dragged.
- also: a window shorter than 740px (a phone with its URL bar showing) can make a 7-10 letter word's screen taller than the window; `#screen-play` is now `touch-action: pan-y` instead of `none`, so the bench pans to the controls, while blocks keep `touch-action: none` and a drag never scrolls. At 360x740 nothing scrolls (a 10-letter word ends at 739px).
- because: DESIGN "Layout" gives the slot and block numbers; the mockup is the only source for how rows split. Fixed spots make the bench look like blocks lying on a table rather than a reflowing list, and fix the B-104 "tray and box grids misalign" note. The tilt has to go through GSAP or Draggable's transform would overwrite a CSS rotate.
- considered: even rows (6 -> 3 + 3, contradicts the mockup); random tilt per render (blocks would jump on every setState); tilting the spot instead of the block (drag offsets would be in rotated coordinates); keeping `touch-action: none` (on a short window the controls of a long word could not be reached).
- reversible: yes.

They are marked interim in the CSS and the template.

- reversible: yes (removed by B-114).

- chose: `console.png` is the last item of the play column (`margin-top: auto`), full safe width (332px at 360 wide, so 59.8px tall by its 972x175 ratio), z-index 2, with one contact shadow. Lever, speaker and dome are `<button>`s holding a stack of their image assets (`lever-up/-down`, `speaker-off/-on/-cap`, `dome-off/-ready/-pressed`); each is centred on its sprite and at least 48x48 (the dome is 84x59). Centres and sprite widths are measured from `play-screen-v5.png` relative to its console and stored in % of the console: lever centre 13.4% / 51%, 11.6% wide (38px at 360); speaker 36.1% / 55.4%, 11.45% (38px); dome 81.5% / 41%, 25.4% (84px; its top rises ~5px above the console as in v5). ## 2026-10-05 — Dome, lever and speaker states from state; `pressed` and `speaking` in state.js (B-114)
- chose: two new state fields. `pressed` is 'dome' for 120ms after a dome press and 'lever' for 250ms after a lever pull (null otherwise); `speaking` is true while the word clip plays. `static/js/controls.js` holds the pure rules (node-tested, `ControlsTests`): `domeAction(current)` is 'check' when every slot is filled and status is 'playing', 'next' after 'correct' or 'revealed', null otherwise; `domeState` is 'pressed' / 'ready' / 'off' from that; `canReset` (status 'playing' or 'wrong'); `leverState`; `speakerState` ('cap' when the word has no `audio`, else 'on' / 'off'); `PRESS_MS = { dome: 120, lever: 250 }`. `renderControls` (a subscriber) puts class `is-<state>` on each button and sets `disabled` when a tap would do nothing; CSS shows the matching image. main.js: a dome tap sets `pressed: 'dome'`, and 120ms later decides the action again (a block may have been taken out) and runs `onCheck` / `onNext` with `pressed: null` in the same setState; a lever tap sets `pressed: 'lever'` and the placement from `returnAll(current)` (drag.js, pure, node-tested) in one setState, and clears `pressed` 250ms later; taps on the console are ignored while `pressed` is set (at most 250ms). The ready glow is `dome-ready.png` over `dome-off.png` with a CSS animation, opacity 0.45 to 1, `1.2s ease-in-out infinite alternate` (1.2s each way, read literally from DESIGN's numbers); under reduced motion it stays at 1.
- chose (after a wrong check): the dome is `off` until a block moves. Any block move (drag, tap, or the lever) turns status 'wrong' back to 'playing' (`afterMove` in drag.js, the same rule as the open B-109 PR #18), so the dome lights again once the slots are full. `onCheck` now only runs from 'playing'.
- because: DESIGN "Controls and states": ready "until pressed or a block is taken out", and only "after a correct answer or a reveal" does it return to ready; pressing again on the arrangement that was just judged wrong would only cost another bulb. Putting `pressed` and `speaking` in state keeps every image a render of state (CLAUDE.md) and gives the press a single guard instead of a module flag. The lever ignores taps after a correct answer or a reveal (blocks are locked then); with nothing placed it still goes down and shakes the rail.
- considered: deciding the dome action at press time (a block taken out during the 120ms would still be checked); swapping image `src` instead of stacking (first swap can flash while loading); a module-global "busy" flag (state in a module global).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-05 — `#next-btn` and `#result-line` removed; no skipping a word (B-114)
- chose: `#next-btn` is deleted from the template and main.js (the dome is also "next", Design v5 and the acceptance). `#result-line`, its `RESULT_TEXT` strings and `renderActions` are deleted too; B-113 had kept the line hidden only so the open B-106 PR would not break, and said B-114 could remove it. Both ids were on the CLAUDE.md protected list (`next-btn` already marked "removed by B-114"). The interim `.icon-btn` buttons and their CSS are gone. With no Next button there is no way to skip an unchecked word: the dome offers "next" only after a correct answer or a reveal. `onNext` still records a missing result as wrong, defensively.
- because: DESIGN "Words on screen" (no result-line text) and "Controls and states" (dome is check and next); a hidden element that main.js keeps writing English into is dead weight. B-106's "Next always advances" acceptance (PR #12, unmerged) predates v5.
- considered: keeping `#result-line` hidden (no text is shown either way, but the strings and a protected id would stay for nothing).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-05 — `audio.js` created with word playback only; overlap with PR #16 (B-114)
- chose: a minimal `static/js/audio.js` (CLAUDE.md: the only place sounds play) with `speakWord(url, onEnd)` and `stopWord()`. A speaker tap sets `speaking: true` and plays `current.audio` (the per-word file from the content JSON) inside the tap handler, so the autoplay policy allows it; `onEnd` (clip ended, failed to load, or playback refused) sets `speaking: false`. A new tap restarts the word; the word clip stops when the word changes, at round end and on Themes (those setStates also clear `speaking`). The playing element is a "DOM cache" module global, never read as truth. The speaker shows `speaker-cap` (taps ignored) only when the word has no `audio` field; a file that fails to play falls back to `speaker-off`. No other sound is added: the voice pools, cues and the mute toggle are B-108.
- because: the acceptance says the speaker plays the word through `audio.js`, which does not exist on main; B-108's `audio.js` (PR #16) forked before B-110..B-113 and brings pools, cues and a mute toggle that are not this item. Creating only what B-114 needs keeps the item small.
- noted: PR #16 also adds `static/js/audio.js`, so whichever lands second resolves an add/add conflict by keeping both parts; B-108's mute must then also stop and gate `speakWord` ("muted means no sound at all"). Headless Chromium refuses `play()` from a script click, so `speaker-on.png` is driven by `setState({ speaking: true })`; the real path (on while playing, off at the end) was checked in a headless browser with autoplay allowed.
- considered: merging PR #16's file now (out of scope, conflicts with B-113's markup); a question issue (CLAUDE.md already designates `audio.js`, so the docs cover it).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-05 — Wrong / reveal / reset motion in a new `feedback.js`; correct flash in `celebrate.js` (B-114)
- chose: `static/js/feedback.js` (the module CLAUDE.md names for one-shot motion keyed on a state transition) is created on main, a subscriber after `renderCard`, with a render cache of the current word's key (round index + word), status, lives, `pressed` and placement. On a lost bulb: that bulb flickers three times in 300ms and stays dark (driven through a `--lit` custom property on the bulb's glow layer, removed afterwards so the `lost` class decides) and the TV picture gets 150ms of static (a `.tv-static` layer of uneven light and dark horizontal bands flickering over the tube while the picture jitters ±2px). Status became 'wrong': blocks in wrong slots wiggle ±6px in 300ms. Status became 'revealed': blocks fly from the slots they were in into the right slots in slot order, 120ms apart, 300ms each with `back.out(1.4)` (landing in a slot); `revealed()` in main.js now keeps a block that is already in its right slot. `pressed` became 'lever': the rail swings ±4px in 200ms and the blocks that were in slots hop back to their start spots (300ms, 30ms apart, 16px arc). celebrate.js adds the warm-white TV flash (`.tv-flash`, cream-hi to amber-hi, fades from 0.9 to 0 in 200ms) to its correct beat; the confetti pieces are restyled in CSS only as 6px glowing sparks in amber, teal glow and cream, still bursting from the TV picture.
- reuse: the wiggle is PR #18's (B-109) code and numbers (`wiggle` 0.3s, 6px, `power1.inOut`, the same 1-2-2-1 swing), ported because B-109 is not on main; the rail shake reuses the same `swing()`. Confetti, slot pulse and star pop are B-110's code unchanged except the added flash. PR #18's error tint on wrong boxes is not ported: DESIGN v5 "Feedback" has no tint.
- details: a flying block gets `position: relative` and a z-index (reveal: later over earlier, so none lands under a block still waiting in its old slot; hop: earlier over later, all in the air together); a `delayedCall` restores position, z-index and opacity when the last lands, because a drag that grabs a block mid-hop kills its tweens. Reduced motion (motion.js): no swing, shake, jitter, flight or hop; wrong blocks dip in opacity, the bulb and the static fade, flown blocks fade in where they land (staggered), and the confetti stays off; `motionFor` now keeps `stagger` under reduced motion (was set to `fade`; equal for the star pop, so B-110 is unchanged).
- noted: the CLAUDE.md `feedback.js` line says its render cache holds "the last status/lives"; this one also holds `pressed` and the last placement, which the reveal and the hop need to know where each block came from. Suggest widening that line.
- considered: doing the reveal FLIP in `onCheck` (motion keyed on a transition belongs to feedback.js); animating the hop from drag.js at tap time (same reason, and the lever is a state transition here); a `<div>` per bulb overlay instead of `--lit` (template change for the same effect).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-05 — 10-letter fit at 360x740: tighter gaps and a third row clear of the robot (B-114)
- chose: budget at 360x740 for a 10-letter word: rail 347-461 (two slot rows), 4px, bench 465-649 (three block rows), at least 4px, console 658-718. To get there, the flex gap in the play column goes from 10px to 4px, `BLOCK_ROW_GAP` from 12px to 10px, and rows from the third on are centred right of `ROBOT_ZONE` (40% of the bench width) in `blockLayout` instead of staggered from centre. Block size (52px), blocks per row (4), slots and the console are unchanged, so no question issue was needed. Keep-out used by the new `TenLetterFitTests` (words of 1-10 letters): the console, the dome, and the robot as in the v5 reference (92px wide, left edge at 7.4% of the console, 75px above it, the top 10px being his brass cap), each block grown by 2px for tilt. Closest margins: the second row's first block clears the robot's cap by ~1px in the test (~4px on screen, `fit-10-robot-proof.png`, where robot-idle is drawn in by `--eval` only; the robot itself is B-115); the third row clears the dome by ~3px and the console by ~8px.
- because: the acceptance requires the fit and allows asking only before changing block size, blocks per row or the console; gaps and the third row's position are B-113 layout choices, not DESIGN numbers. In v5 the short last row also sits right of the robot.
- noted for B-115: there is no room for the robot's 20px pop-up above the second block row on 7-10 letter words; he must sit under the bench blocks in z-order (CHARACTER: he never covers the blocks).
- considered: 8px row gap (adjacent rows then come within 2px, below B-113's 4px spacing test); smaller blocks or five per row for long words (excluded by the acceptance without asking).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-05 — Corrections from the B-114 review, round 1 (B-114)
- chose: in `static/js/audio.js`, `wordClip` is now described as a handle to the playing word clip: not game state and not a DOM cache. audio.js reads it only to tell whether an ended clip is still the current one; no other module reads it; `state.speaking` is the truth. ## 2026-10-09 — The robot: one moment table in `character.js`, played on state transitions (B-115)
- chose: `static/js/character.js` holds the whole robot. `MOMENTS` maps each moment of CHARACTER.md's "Moment map" to its steps (`{ pose, move }`, pose and movement change together) and a sound; `MOVES` holds the movements; `snapshot(state)` and `momentsFor(prev, now)` pick the moments between two states; `stepsFor`, `stepDelays`, `moveFor` and `idleWait` are the rest of the pure part (node-tested, `CharacterTests`). `renderRobot` is a subscriber like feedback.js: a render cache of the last snapshot and of when it last changed his pose; on a moment it plays the steps `CHANGE_MS` (300ms) apart, the first no sooner than 300ms after the previous change, through `gsap.delayedCall` (a newer moment kills the steps an older one still had waiting, via `gsap.killTweensOf(applyStep)`, so no timer handle is kept). The pose is class `is-<pose>` on `.robot` over a stack of the six pose images (as the console controls do); the movement is a GSAP slide of `.robot-body` in `yPercent`. No other module decides a pose.
- because: the acceptance asks for one table (moment → pose, movement, sound) and no pose logic elsewhere; CHARACTER "Presence" says never more than one change per 300ms, which needs a short queue keyed on time. Keying on transitions, as feedback.js and celebrate.js do, means a re-render in the same state replays nothing.
- considered: keeping the pose in state (it is set on timers after the transition, so a subscriber would have to call setState from inside a render); swapping one `<img>`'s `src` (can flash on the first swap, which is why B-114 stacks the control images).
- noted: CLAUDE.md calls `feedback.js` "the only place one-shot motion keyed on a state transition starts". The robot's slides also start on transitions, but in `character.js`, because the B-115 acceptance puts his pose and pop/sink/peek movement there and allows "no pose logic elsewhere". Suggest that the CLAUDE.md line say "(the robot's moves are character.js)".
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-09 — Readings of the moment map (B-115)
- chose:
  - Round start and "picture shown" happen together: idle with a pop, then thinking 300ms later. Each later word is "picture shown": thinking.
  - "Block placed" is any block move on the word (into a slot, back to the bench, the reset lever): idle. "Eyes follow" is not done; the eyes are drawn into the sprites, and moving them would mean drawing eyes in CSS (DESIGN layer model).
  - "All slots filled ... for 2 rounds": excited (closest pose to pointing at the dome, CHARACTER "Poses") in the first two rounds since the page was loaded, idle from the third. New state `roundsStarted` counts them; it is not stored.
  - "Wrong check: confused, peek (sometimes)": he peeks when the miss leaves one lives bulb lit (the second miss); on the first miss he is only confused. Deterministic, so it can be tested and shot.
  - "Third miss: oops → happy as blocks fly in": oops at the reveal, happy 300ms later while the reveal fly-in (120ms apart) is still landing.
  - "3 correct in a row" counts within the round: the third, fourth and fifth correct in a row each get the double pop.
  - "Kid idle 10s": thinking, then the sink 300ms later; the kid's next input is a `wake` moment (idle with a pop, CHARACTER "pops back up on the kid's next action"). It counts in any status of the word, also while the dome waits for "next".
  - "Round end": the moment is in the table and picked (happy for 2-3 stars, idle for 0-1), but the robot only lives on the play screen, so nothing shows it until a screen does.
  - Sound column: data only. Playing the voice clips is B-108's acceptance (pools in `audio.js` plus the mute toggle, and "muted means no sound plays at all"), and there is no mute toggle on main yet; B-108 can read the clip per moment from this table.
- because: CHARACTER.md leaves these choices open; each option above is the one closest to its words and testable.
- considered: a random peek (not reproducible in tests or screenshots); a streak that runs across rounds (needs history outside the round); playing the existing clips now (sound with no way to mute, and a second add/add conflict with PR #16).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-09 — Movement numbers, reduced motion (B-115)
- chose: in CSS px at 360 wide, positive down. Pop: up 20px in 180ms `power2.out`, then settle back in 120ms. Double pop: two pops. Sink: down 61px in 400ms `power2.out`, which leaves 14px of him (his brass cap and the top of his head). Peek: down 18px in 150ms, which leaves 57px (the console edge crosses just under his eyes), held 600ms, back in 200ms. A step with no movement brings him back to rest in 200ms if a move left him elsewhere. Every move except the sink ends at rest. The numbers convert to `yPercent` of the 120px sprite so they scale with it. Under `prefers-reduced-motion` the moves become a set to rest: pose swaps only (CHARACTER), no fade.
- because: CHARACTER "Presence" gives ~20px / 180ms / ease-out, 400ms and 600ms; the depths come from the sprites (eye rows 107-167 of 330 in robot-confused, cap at the top). The settle time keeps a pop inside one 300ms change. No overshoot: DESIGN keeps `back.out` for block snaps and star pops.
- considered: an opacity fade under reduced motion (DESIGN's general rule; CHARACTER, which governs the robot, says pose swaps only).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-09 — Kid-idle timer: `lastInput` and `idleBeat` in state, monotonic clock (B-115)
- chose: state gets `lastInput` (`performance.now()` of the kid's last input on the word, or of its start) and `idleBeat` (null until 10s pass without input, 'sunk' until the next input, 'over' after it, so the beat comes once per word), both reset when a word starts. main.js listens for `pointerdown` and `keydown` on the document (capture phase, so it sees an input before a block or control handles it). On the play screen, an input sets `lastInput` while the beat has not come, and wakes him (`idleBeat: 'over'`) while he is sunk. After that it does nothing. `armIdle()` starts with each word: a `setTimeout` of 10s that asks `idleWait(state, now)` (pure, in character.js) how long is left, re-arms for that, and sets `idleBeat: 'sunk'` at 0. It stops when the word or the screen changes, or when the beat has come. The word it belongs to is the timer's own closure, not a module global.
- because: CLAUDE.md puts anything that is not a render/DOM cache in state, and the acceptance says "driven from state". A drag synthesised with pointer events still places the block, so the capture listener's setState does not disturb Draggable. While checking the 10s beat, the page's `Date.now()` ran 2.3s ahead of `performance.now()` (the WSL wall clock stepped). A wall-clock timer fires early after such a jump and late after a backward one; `performance.now()` is monotonic.
- considered: a module-global timer handle cleared on every input (state outside state.js, CLAUDE.md); counting only inputs that change state (a tap on the empty bench is input too, "any input pops him back"); `Date.now()`.
- reversible: yes.

- chose: `.robot` sits inside `.console` (first child), so it is placed against the console's own box and `--console-w`. It is a clip window from 130px above the console down to 95% of its height (console.png's opaque bottom edge), 160px wide, `overflow: hidden`, `pointer-events: none`, and `z-index: -1`. That puts him in `#screen-play`'s stacking context (`isolation: isolate`) right above the lab picture and below the rail, the bench blocks and the console. For that, `#screen-play .console` loses B-114's `z-index: 2` (a z-index would make the console a stacking context and lift him above the bench with it). The console still paints over resting blocks because it comes after them in the tree; blocks in flight (z-index 10+) and a dragged block (Draggable's z-index boost) still go over it, as before. Sizes are in `--rpx` (1px at the 324px console): every pose (all 330px tall at one scale) is drawn 120px tall, its top 75px above the console. Each pose is shifted so its visor centre (measured per sprite: idle 150, thinking 149, confused 132, excited 164, happy 148, oops 144) is 72.6px from the console's left edge (22.4%), so his head stays put when the pose changes. One contact shadow (`drop-shadow`, DESIGN). New `RobotLayoutTests`:

  - a pixel test on the six sprites at 360x740. He shows down to the console edge and stays in the console's left half. At rest he is clear of every block of 1-10 letter words and of the rail. At the top of a pop he is clear of 1-6 letter words, while 7-10 letter words' second row covers his cap (the reason for the z-order, BACKLOG B-115).

The scale comes from v5's visor (56.7px wide against 157px in robot-idle). v5 shows about 71px; the docs' 75px was used, as in B-114's keep-out. The window clips a sunk robot so nothing of him shows under the console's lower edge.

Suggested CLAUDE.md "Frontend architecture" bullet (not edited here): "`character.js` is the only place the robot's poses and moves are decided: one table (`MOMENTS`: moment → steps of pose and movement, and a sound) and pure helpers (`snapshot`, `momentsFor`, `stepsFor`, `stepDelays`, `moveFor`, `idleWait`) tested through `node`; `renderRobot` is a subscriber with a render cache of the last snapshot and the time of its last change, and never mutates state. main.js owns the idle timer and the input listener that feed `lastInput` and `idleBeat`."

## 2026-10-10 — M2 levels and hints (human decisions, planning chat)
- chose: three levels picked with bulb icons, default easy. Hear the word: easy any time; normal and hard only after the word is answered (correct or revealed), and before that the speaker plays something else (a robot "hmm no") instead of the word. Easy gives the first letter and starts with short words. Lever on easy after a wrong check: first pull drops only the wrong letters and the right ones stay; pulling again starts the word over. Normal and hard: one pull drops all. Help grows per miss on every level: 1st miss shows wrong letters, 2nd miss the robot places one correct letter (OK on hard too), 3rd miss reveals. Hard adds 2 confusing extra letters. Missed words come back in the next round; stickers only for correct words. Moving up a level is suggested after strong rounds, never forced. A back button leaves a round without asking. The robot will become a cut-out puppet once clean parts exist. The earlier "Mode 1 / Mode 2" idea is dropped in favour of the per-miss help ladder.
- because: the human answered the levels-and-hints proposal: (1) hear the word after answering on every level, (2) keep correct letters only when the kid pulls the lever, with a way to start over, (3) a given letter on the 2nd miss is fine on hard, (4) extra letters on hard are good; without word audio before answering on hard, the speaker should play something else.
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-11 — Sounds come from the moment table: audio.js hears state; "Block placed" split into placed / returned (B-108)

It runs on every screen, so the round-end tada plays although the robot is drawn only on the play screen. In `character.js` "Block placed" is split: `blockPlaced` (a slot now holds a block it did not hold before: from the bench, from another slot, or swapping one out; swipe) and `blockReturned` (only slots emptied: a tap or drag back to the bench, or the reset lever; silent). Both are idle with no move, as before. `filledEarly` / `filled` get the swipe, because the block that fills the last slot always went into a slot. New pure helpers `blockEntered(before, after)` (on the snapshot's `placed` string) and `soundsFor(moments)`; the empty snapshot `NONE` is exported so both subscribers start from it. The reveal fly-in stays `thirdMiss` (error), not a swipe.

## 2026-10-11 — Pools, voice and effect channels, first-gesture unlock (B-108)
- chose: `FILES` in audio.js spells out the clips of each pool (`correct/`, `error/`, `celebration/tada.mp3`, `swipe/`; the browser cannot list a directory); a test checks the list against the files on disk. URLs are built with `encodeURIComponent` (`hmm%20no.mp3`). `init()` (main.js, at start-up) makes one `Audio` per clip with `preload = 'auto'` (25 clips, about 1 MB) and plays nothing. `play(kind)` picks a random clip (`pickIndex`). It does not remember the last pick, so a line can come twice in a row; remembering it would be state in a module global. Two channels: voice (`correct`, `error`, `tada`), where a new clip first stops every voice clip, and fx (`swipe`), which overlaps anything. A voice clip that comes while the target word plays (`state.speaking`) is dropped, not queued. `speakWord` stops the voice before the word starts. Unlock: the first `pointerup`, `touchend`, `click` or `keydown` (capture; these count as a user activation in Chrome and a user gesture in Safari, unlike a touch `pointerdown`) starts every clip element-muted and pauses it once it runs. A clip that `play()` claims in the meantime is unmuted by it and left playing (the release checks `el.muted`). Every `play()` promise is caught, so a refused start is silent and logs nothing.
- because: DESIGN "Sound" and CHARACTER "Rules": one voice clip at a time, a new one cuts the previous, except the target word, which "is always spoken clean and complete". The swipe is a block sound, not the robot's voice. iOS Safari grants playback per media element, and the check and round-end clips start from the dome's 120ms timer, not inside the tap.
- verified on this branch (headless Chromium with `--autoplay-policy=document-user-activation-required`, real touch input through CDP, `HTMLMediaElement.prototype.play` logged; a throwaway probe, not committed): no `play()` on load; the console holds only the existing manifest-icon and favicon 404s and Chrome's `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` deprecation warning, nothing from audio. The first tap primes all 25 clips muted. Over a full round (cat, deer, rabbit, dog, fish): one swipe per block into a slot, the last one included; nothing for a block tapped back to the bench or for the lever; an error clip on each wrong check and on the reveal; a correct clip on each correct check, the fifth word's streak included; the tada once at round end and nothing on a re-render there. A correct check while the word was playing stayed silent. No `play()` promise was rejected.
- considered: Web Audio with decoded buffers (more code; the acceptance is about Audio pools); one element per channel that swaps `src` (a fetch before every play); queueing a voice clip until the word ends (more state for a rare case); no unlock at all (all cues but the dome's already run inside a gesture).
- noted: `tools/shot.mjs`'s ENOTEMPTY failure while removing its temporary profile (BACKLOG "Later") still shows up with the preloaded clips; the PNG is written before it. The tool is not changed in this item.
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-11 — Mute: `state.muted` in `flip.muted`, read and written only by audio.js; muted silences everything (B-108)
- chose: `state.muted` (default false). Only audio.js reads or writes localStorage `flip.muted`, through `loadMuted()` / `saveMuted(muted)`, both in try/catch: 'true' is muted; missing, anything else, or storage that cannot be read is sound on (DESIGN: on by default). main.js sets the flag from storage at start-up and saves it when the toggle is tapped. `play()` and `speakWord()` do nothing when muted (`speakWord` calls its `onEnd` at once); `renderSound` pauses the word clip and every pooled clip when the flag turns on; `onMute` clears `speaking` in the same setState, so the speaker goes back to off. A speaker tap while muted is ignored (nothing would play), and the speaker keeps its normal look.
- because: the acceptance says "Muted means no sound plays at all". stickers.js is the precedent for one module owning a localStorage key.
- verified (same probe): after the toggle, `flip.muted` is 'true' and the button has `is-muted` and `aria-pressed="true"`; a whole word placed and checked plays nothing; after a reload the game comes back muted and a speaker tap plays nothing and leaves `speaking` false; unmuting writes 'false' and the next block swipes.
- considered: `speaker-cap` while muted (DESIGN keeps the cap for a mode that hides the speaker); writing the flag from the subscriber (a side effect inside a render).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-11 — Pooled sounds play through Web Audio, not <audio> elements (yaron/audio-webaudio)
- chose: `static/js/audio.js` plays the robot's clips and the block swipe through one Web Audio `AudioContext` instead of preloaded `<audio>` elements. `init()` fetches every clip once; the first gesture (pointerup / touchend / click / keydown, capture phase) creates the context, resumes it, plays one silent sample and decodes the clips into `AudioBuffer`s; `play(kind)` starts a decoded buffer at once. The gesture listeners stay attached so a context suspended or interrupted after an app switch resumes on the next tap. A clip that fails to download or decode is left out of its pool; no Web Audio means silence, not an error. On iOS 17+ `navigator.audioSession.type = 'playback'` keeps the clips audible with the silent switch on, as the `<audio>` clips were (feature-detected; older iOS keeps the default). The target word stays an `<audio>` element (`speakWord`), started by the kid's own tap. Supersedes the `pools` entry above (B-108); CLAUDE.md's audio.js bullet now names the new handles (`ctx`, `bytes`, `buffers`, `live`).
- because: playtest on iPhone (Brave, WebKit underneath): the swipe lagged visibly behind the drop and some sounds never played. `<audio>` on iOS has a few hundred ms from `play()` to sound, ignores `preload` (a clip's first play waits for its download), and the start-25-muted-elements unlock is refused for some elements, which then stay silent until a reload. The swipe's state change already fires on the drop itself (drag.js `onDragEnd` → setState → renderSound), so the delay was the playback engine. No new dependency.
- not done: `correct/congratulations.mp3` (0.54 s) and `correct/correct3.mp3` (0.28 s) start with silence, which still delays the voice when one of them is picked; CLAUDE.md forbids trimming sound files in M1.
- reversible: yes (audio.js playback section only; its exported functions are unchanged).

## 2026-10-12 — Mute glyph: B-108's icons in a tight viewBox, 14px tall; switch images stacked and swapped by opacity; no press scale (B-108b)
- chose: the glyph keeps B-108's two paths, cream, as code: the speaker with one wave, and the speaker with a cross. Their shared viewBox becomes `2.4 4.2 19.8 15.6`, the bounding box of both icons including their strokes, round caps and joins. So `height: 14px` is the drawn glyph's height (width `calc(14px * 19.8 / 15.6)` = 17.8), and the speaker sits at the same place in both icons. `overflow: visible` keeps the anti-aliased edges. The contact shadow is scaled to the smaller glyph: `drop-shadow(1px 1px 1px var(--shadow-contact))`. The two switch images are stacked absolutely in one 60px box, with one contact shadow for the stack: `drop-shadow(1px 2px 2px var(--shadow-contact))`. That is the console sprites' `2px 3px 3px` scaled down to a 20px-tall sprite; the images have no baked-in cast shadow. They are swapped by opacity from `is-muted`, using the same pattern as the lever and the dome (`.ctl .sprite img`): knob right (`switch-on`) at rest, knob left (`switch-off`) while muted. The selectors are chained through `.switch` so they outrank `.mute-btn .switch img { opacity: 0 }`. Both images are in the markup, so both load with the page and the first toggle does not flash: on this branch both are `complete` with `naturalWidth` 180 before the first tap. The swap is instant, like the lever and the dome. B-108's `:active` scale on the glyph is removed: the knob jumping is the feedback, and no scene sprite scales on press. The CSS cache-buster goes `?v=14` -> `?v=15`.
- because: BACKLOG B-108b says the images are "stacked and swapped by class (as the lever and dome do), so there is no flash on the first toggle", with the contact shadow in CSS. DESIGN "Assets" gives every sprite a soft contact shadow. DESIGN "Motion" specifies no knob slide, and the two images are the only states.
- considered: a knob slide (would need the knob as its own image or a crossfade; neither is in DESIGN); keeping the press scale on the glyph only (half the control would move).
- tests: `MuteToggleTests` gets three new tests. `test_glyph_then_switch_images_in_one_button` checks the markup. `test_glyph_is_about_14px` checks the shared viewBox and the glyph's size. `test_switch_images_are_stacked_and_swapped_by_class` checks that the assets are 180x59, the box is 60px wide at 59/180, the images are stacked, the opacity swap by `is-muted`, the contact shadow, no display swap, and that main.js never names the images. `test_toggle_is_at_least_48px_and_shows_one_icon` becomes `test_tap_area_is_at_least_84x48_and_shows_one_glyph`, which also checks that glyph + gap + switch fit the padded box. ## 2026-10-13 — Leaving a round: its new stickers come out of the book, the round is cleared, only the word clip stops, no robot moment (B-201)
- chose: `onPlayBack` (main.js) does nothing off the play screen. On the play screen it takes out of the book every sticker this round added: each result with `correct` and `newSticker` goes through the new `stickers.remove(theme, word)`. That returns true when the book had the word, writes nothing otherwise, and drops a theme left empty. A sticker the book already had before the round (`newSticker: false`) stays. It then calls the existing `onThemes`, which stops the word clip (`stopWord`, before the setState) and, in one setState, goes to the theme screen and clears the theme, words, results, current word, `pressed`, `speaking` and `idleBeat`. No confirmation. Nothing else changes: the robot's voice and effect clips already playing (a correct or error clip, the swipe) end on their own, and no moment fires (CHARACTER.md's moment map has no row for leaving). The pending timers find the round gone: the dome's 120ms press checks the screen, the lever's checks `pressed`, the idle timer ends off the play screen, and renderRobot drops his waiting steps when the screen changes. The stopped word clip never calls its `onEnd` (audio.js `done` checks `wordClip`).
- because: the acceptance says "no stars, no stickers, no results kept". Stickers are written to the book when a word is checked correct (B-111, `onCheck`), not at round end, so a dropped round keeps none only if those are taken back. The book is touched only through stickers.js (CLAUDE.md), hence `remove` there. The acceptance names only the word clip; the robot's clips are short and cutting them is not asked for.
- considered: keeping the stickers already earned in the round (contradicts "no stickers ... kept"); deferring the sticker write to round end (changes B-111's tested flow, outside this item); stopping every sound on back (not asked; audio.js has no exported "stop all" except mute); a robot "bye" moment (not in CHARACTER.md).
- checked (this branch, 360x740, headless Chromium with autoplay allowed, throwaway probe outside the repo): with the book seeded `{animals: [cat]}`, a seeded round of animals, word 1 (wolf) solved and checked through the real dome (book `[cat, wolf]`, `newSticker: true`), word 2 (fish) with one block placed and the speaker tapped (`play fish.mp3`, `speaking: true`). A click on `#play-back-btn` then gives `pause fish.mp3`, screen `theme`, theme null, words `[]`, round `{index 0, size 5, results []}`, an empty current word, `speaking: false`, `pressed: null`, `idleBeat: null`, book `{animals: [cat]}`, the button `display: none`, and `body[data-screen="theme"]`.
- tests: `PlayBackTests` covers the markup (the gallery's SVG, no text, between the logo and the toggle), the CSS (hidden except on the play screen, at least 48x48, tappable, the margins, cream with no face) and the wiring (`onPlayBack` removes only `newSticker` words, ends in `onThemes`, no `confirm(`; `onThemes` stops the word before its setState and clears the round). `StickerBookTests.test_remove_takes_out_only_that_word_and_drops_an_empty_theme` covers `remove`. `CharacterTests.test_back_to_themes_is_no_moment_and_a_new_round_starts_fresh` and `AudioTests.test_back_to_themes_stops_the_word_and_plays_nothing` guard the transition from eight and four points of a word. On the pre-B-201 code 4 of these 6 fail (the character and audio ones guard existing `onThemes` behaviour). 136 tests in all, passing.
- screenshots (`.agent/screenshots/B-201/`, tools/shot.mjs at 360x740, the same seeded round): `play-back.png` (word 2/5, one block placed, the arrow in the header), `theme-after-back.png` (after a click on the arrow: the theme screen, no arrow), `stickers-after-back.png` (the book after that: 1/14, cat lit, wolf not kept), `round-end-no-back.png` (the round-end screen has no arrow), `header-zoom-x3.png` (CSS x 100-360, y 20-86 of `play-back.png` at 3x, with the back box and the toggle's tap area dashed on the crop only, made from a throwaway page outside the repo).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-13 — Level in state: `state.level` in `flip.level`, owned by a new `level.js`; the round keeps its own `round.level` (B-202)
- chose: a new `static/js/level.js`, no DOM and no module state, is the only module that names localStorage `flip.level`: `loadLevel(storage)` / `saveLevel(level, storage)`, both in try/catch, as audio.js does for `flip.muted` and stickers.js for the book. Storage defaults to `globalThis.localStorage`, looked up inside the try (reading `localStorage` itself throws when the browser blocks storage). A missing value, anything other than `'easy'`, `'normal'` or `'hard'` (case and spaces count), or storage that cannot be read is `'easy'`; `saveLevel` writes only a real level, and a failed write is ignored. `state.level` defaults to `'easy'`; main.js sets it from `loadLevel()` at start-up, right after the mute flag (its own setState line). A tap on a level button (`onLevel`, theme screen only) saves it and sets it.
- chose: "fixed for the round once a theme is tapped" is held in state: `startRound` reads `state.level` before it fetches the theme's data file and writes it as `round.level`; nothing else writes it until the next `startRound` (onCheck and onNext spread `...round`). Leaving a round (`onThemes`) sets it back to `null`, the initial value in state.js. B-203…B-207 should take their rules from `levelRules(state.round.level)`, not from `state.level`. Checked in the browser: tap normal, tap a theme, tap hard before the fetch resolves: the round plays at normal and the next one at hard; a click on a level button during play (the row is hidden there) changes nothing.
- chose: `levelRules(level)` returns a new object each call from one table: easy `{ hearBefore: true, firstLetterGiven: true, shortWordsFirst: true, leverDropsWrongFirst: true, distractors: 0 }`, normal all false and 0, hard as normal with `distractors: 2`. `distractors` is the number of extra letters (B-207 asks for 2), not a flag, so B-207 reads the count from the table. Anything that is not a level gets easy's rules (so `round.level` null, between rounds, reads as easy). Also exported: `LEVELS`, `DEFAULT_LEVEL`, `LEVEL_KEY`, `toLevel(value)`.
- because: CLAUDE.md wants state in state.js only, one module per localStorage key, and pure helpers tested through node. Copying the level into the round makes the acceptance's rule true by construction, not only because the picker is hidden during play; B-209 (two 3-star rounds "on the same level") can compare `round.level` too. Reading it before the fetch is what "once a theme is tapped" says.
- considered: reading `state.level` in later items (true today only because the picker is on the theme screen alone); putting `loadLevel`/`saveLevel` in main.js (main.js has DOM and no other storage, and the tests check it never names `localStorage`); `distractors` as a boolean (B-207 would need the 2 elsewhere); throwing on a bad level in `levelRules`, as `starsFor` does (the acceptance makes a bad value easy).
- note for B-209: `onLevel` ignores taps off the theme screen; the round-end "next level" button will need its own handler that saves and sets the level before `startRound`.
- not edited (docs change only with the human's approval): suggested CLAUDE.md "Frontend architecture" line after `stickers.js`: "`level.js` is the only place localStorage `flip.level` is read or written (`loadLevel`/`saveLevel`); `levelRules(level)` is the per-level rules table; no DOM, no module state; a Django test runs it through `node`." Suggested protected ids: `level-picker`, `level-easy`, `level-normal`, `level-hard` (main.js keys on them).
- reversible: yes.
