## 2026-10-13 — Speaker per level: `speakerAction(current, level)` on the round's level; a held-back tap counts `state.lockedTaps` (B-203)
- chose: a new pure rule in `static/js/controls.js`, `speakerAction(current, level)`: null when there is no word or no audio file (the speaker is capped, as before); 'word' once the word is answered (status 'correct' or 'revealed') on every level; before that 'word' when `levelRules(level).hearBefore` (easy) and 'locked' otherwise (normal, hard). controls.js imports `levelRules` from level.js (both pure). main.js `onSpeak` asks it with `state.round.level`, the round's copy written by `startRound` (B-202), never `state.level`. 'locked' adds one to a new state field `lockedTaps` (default 0, counted over the session, written only there) and returns: no word clip starts and `speaking` stays false. 'word' plays the word as before. `speakerState` is unchanged, so the speaker shows the same `speaker-off` image whether or not it holds the word back, and renderControls still disables it only when capped (a held-back tap must reach main.js). No lock icon, no new image or class.
- chose: each held-back tap must be a state transition, or a second tap would replay nothing (the robot and the sound play moments only on a change they detect against their caches). A counter changes on every tap, so tap 1, 2, 3 are each the moment; a re-render is not. It is never reset: only a change matters, and a new word or round is a 'picture' / 'roundStart' moment before the count is looked at.
- chose (mute): a held-back tap while muted still counts. The robot does his confused "no" with no sound, because mute is about sound (BACKLOG "Mute (B-108) silences all of it"; the robot's poses play while muted on every other moment too). renderSound plays nothing while muted. A tap that would play the word stays ignored while muted, as B-108 left it.
- because: BACKLOG B-203 and the B-202 entry ("B-203…B-207 should take their rules from `levelRules(state.round.level)`"); CLAUDE.md keeps state in state.js, the controls' rules pure in controls.js, sounds in audio.js and the robot in character.js, each keyed on transitions.
- considered: `pressed: 'speaker'` for a few hundred ms, as the dome and lever do (it would block the dome and lever after a speaker tap, and a tap inside that window would be no transition); a timestamp (not deterministic in tests); resetting the count per word (more setState lines for nothing the moments need); ignoring a held-back tap while muted (a muted kid on normal would see nothing happen); a capped or disabled speaker on normal (the acceptance says it looks the same).
- checked (this branch, 360x740, headless Chromium with real touch taps through CDP and the page's AudioBufferSourceNode / HTMLMediaElement starts logged; a throwaway probe outside the repo): normal, before answering: each tap starts `error/hmm no.mp3` (a second tap stops the first and starts it again), no word clip, `speaking` false, the robot `is-confused` and turning; after a wrong check the same; after a correct check the word clip plays and the speaker is `is-on`. Muted: a held-back tap starts nothing and the robot still turns; muted after the answer: nothing, `speaking` false. Easy: the word plays before answering and after a wrong check. Hard: held back before answering, the word plays once revealed. Back to themes stops a playing word clip as before.
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-13 — The `speakerLocked` moment: confused with a small head-shake, a turn about his neck (B-203)
- chose: `MOMENTS.speakerLocked = { steps: [{ pose: 'confused', move: 'shake' }], sound: 'locked' }`. momentsFor() returns it when `snapshot().lockedTaps` changed on the same word (after the new round / new word / status checks; NONE has `lockedTaps: 0`). The new move `shake` turns `.robot-body` about his neck: +4.5°, -4.5°, +4.5°, -4.5°, +4.5°, 0, 90ms each, `sine.inOut`, 540ms in all, at rest height. Segments gain an optional `rotation` (degrees); `slide()` sets `rotation: s.rotation || 0` on every segment, so any other move (or the reduced-motion set) turns him upright again. The pivot is CSS on `.robot-body`: `transform-origin: calc(72.6 * var(--rpx)) calc(75 * var(--rpx))`, the visor centre's column (every pose is aligned on it) where the console's top edge crosses him at rest (measured at 360x740: 90.6, 659.7, the console's top). His head swings and his shoulders stay at the edge; the turn never reaches past the 160px window (about 6px at the top of his cap). `prefers-reduced-motion`: the pose swap only, no turn (moveFor gives the rest set, as for every move). CSS cache-buster `?v=17` -> `?v=18`.
- chose (spacing, CHARACTER "Presence"): nothing new. renderRobot already plays a moment's first step no sooner than 300ms after his last change and replaces steps still waiting, so a quick run of taps gives at most one shake per 300ms; each tap's "hmm no" starts at once and cuts the one before (one voice clip at a time, DESIGN "Sound"). A tap that also wakes a sunk robot is two moments: its pointerdown wakes him (idle, pop), its click shakes his head 300ms later.
- because: BACKLOG B-203 asks for "confused pose, a small head-shake". CHARACTER.md "Presence" says movement is a vertical slide and nothing else, but a vertical move would read as a nod; the item is newer and names the shake, and B-210's moment list has "speaker locked (head shake, shrug)". The human-approved puppet page (`design/reference/robot-puppet.html`, `shake()`) turns the head by 9° about the neck in 90ms swings with `sine.inOut`; with one sprite the closest is to turn the whole sprite about the same point (only head and shoulders show), and the 9° is split ±4.5° about upright so the "no" goes both ways and ends at rest.
- considered: a horizontal shake of a few px (reads as a jitter of the whole robot, and the reference turns); the reference's one-sided 0↔9° (on a still sprite it looks like a twitch to one side); no movement (the acceptance asks for the shake); waiting for B-210's separate head.
- not edited (docs change only with the human's approval): suggested CHARACTER.md wording for "Presence": "Movement is a vertical slide, nothing else, except the small head-shake (a turn about the neck) of 'Speaker locked'", and a "Moment map" row: "Speaker locked (normal and hard, before the word is answered) | confused, small head-shake | hmm no (placeholder) | every tap".
- reversible: yes.

- chose: audio.js `FILES.locked = { dir: 'error', names: ['hmm no.mp3'] }`, channel 'voice' (it is the robot's voice: it cuts his clip playing and never starts over the target word). `init()` keeps one download per URL for the call (a local map) and `decodeAll()` one decode per download, giving the buffer to every pool slot that names the file: `decodeAudioData` takes over the bytes it is given, so the shared bytes could not be decoded twice. Still 25 fetches and 25 decodes. The error pool keeps "hmm no" (it is still one of the wrong-check lines). No sound file is added, trimmed or re-encoded.

- tests: `ControlsTests` (`speakerAction` per level and status, null round level as easy, capped / no word; the held-back speaker's look). `CharacterTests` (the table and sounds with `speakerLocked`; every tap a moment, the second too, after a wrong check, a re-render none, a sunk robot wakes first; a new word or round wins; the shake's numbers, reduced motion, no other move turns; `test_moves` now allows `rotation` on the shake only). `AudioTests` (the `locked` pool and channel; "hmm no" fetched and decoded once and played by both pools; renderSound says "hmm no" on each count change, cuts the last, nothing on a re-render or while muted, also after a wrong check). New `SpeakerPerLevelTests` (state default; onSpeak reads `state.round.level`, counts before the mute check, plays no word when held back; only onSpeak writes the count; the speaker stays enabled with its three images; the neck pivot in CSS). On this branch's pre-B-203 static files 15 of them fail (14 failures, 1 error). 165 tests in all, passing.
- screenshots (`.agent/screenshots/B-203/`, 360x740): `speaker-locked-robot.png` (normal, word 1/5, after a click on the speaker: confused, at +4.5° of the shake; the robot's slide timeline paused at 90ms in the `--eval`, the app unchanged), `speaker-locked-muted.png` (the same while muted: the switch knob left, no sound, the robot still shakes his head), `speaker-answered-normal.png` (normal, the word checked correct, then the speaker: `speaker-on`), `speaker-easy.png` (easy, before answering, the speaker: `speaker-on`, the robot thinking); `speaker-locked-zoom-x3.png` (CSS x 10-190, y 560-700 of three frames at 3x: rest, +4.5°, -4.5°, the pivot dotted; made from a throwaway page outside the repo). The two `speaker-on` shots use a throwaway copy of tools/shot.mjs that adds `userGesture: true` to its `Runtime.evaluate`, since headless Chromium refuses a word clip started by a scripted click (the speaker went straight back to off); viewport, scale and capture are tools/shot.mjs's.

## 2026-10-15 — Easy start: the given letter is a locked slot; its block is `given` and has no bench spot (B-204)
- chose: "locked" lives on the slot: `current.placed[i] = { letter, tileId, locked: true }`. A locked slot takes nothing and gives nothing back, and that is the whole rule, in drag.js: `isLocked(slot)`; the new pure `placeIn(current, tileId, index)` and `takeOut(current, tileId)` (what `placeTile` / `returnToTray` did, returning the new current or null, the two now only commit it) refuse a locked slot as target, a locked block as the one to move and a locked block to tap out; `returnAll` (the lever) keeps every locked slot and its block's `used`; the drop hit test skips locked slots, so a block dropped across the locked slot and the next one lands in the next one. Its block cannot be dragged: card.js passes the locked tile ids to `setDragEnabled(on, locked)`, which now sets every Draggable on each render (`enabled(want)` only when it differs) and keeps those disabled; the module global `enabled` is gone. card.js toggles class `locked` on the slot for the look (game.css), as it does `filled`.
- chose: the given letter's block is a tray tile like any other, with `used: true` and `given: true`: card.js lays out bench spots only for the tray tiles without `given`, so the bench shows the other n-1 blocks with no gap (duck: c, u, k), and a 7-letter word has 6 blocks in 4 + 2. `round.js makeWord` (next entry) sets it on easy (`levelRules(level).firstLetterGiven`): the first tray tile with the word's first letter (for "dad" one d is given and the other stays on the bench). The reveal keeps a right slot's object as it is (`{ ...p }`), so the given letter stays locked through a reveal; after a correct check or a reveal it keeps the look (still the given letter).
- chose (nothing else changes): the dome rules are untouched, so the dome is off at the start of an easy word and lights only when every slot is full, and a full word still waits for the check (a word whose only letter is given would start full and wait too; none exists in the content). The robot and the sounds see the given letter as part of the new word ("picture" moment, no swipe); the kid's first block is "block placed" as before. The lever with only the given letter in place still goes down and shakes the rail, as it does today with nothing placed. Normal and hard: `firstLetterGiven` is false, nothing is locked, and every path is as before.
- for B-205 / B-206: they lock right letters or the hint letter by setting `locked: true` on those slots; their blocks keep their bench spots (not `given`), so a later unlock is the slot going back to null. B-205's second pull must keep the `given` one and send back the other locked ones; today `returnAll` keeps every locked slot, which is all B-204 needs, and B-205's `leverResult` replaces that rule.
- because: BACKLOG B-204 ("starts in the first slot, locked: it cannot be dragged, the lever never removes it ... drawn as placed with a small lit-edge locked look ... Its tile is not on the bench"; "the dome lights when all slots are full, as now"); CLAUDE.md keeps state in state.js and the rules pure and node-tested. A flag on the slot goes away with the slot entry, so nothing has to be unlocked separately.
- considered: a separate `current.locked` array (two arrays to keep in step on every move); leaving the given block out of `tray` (card.js keys its tiles off the tray, and the reveal builds from the word's own tiles); the lock on the tray tile (B-205's locks belong to a slot, not a block); leaving the given block a bench spot (an empty gap where its block would lie, against "its tile is not on the bench").
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-15 — Easy word pick: the easy-tagged words first, at random, then the shortest others; the round plays them in random order (B-204)
- chose: `pickWords(data, size, level)` in the new round.js. Normal and hard (`shortWordsFirst` false): the theme's words shuffled and cut at five, exactly what main.js did before (a test checks it against the same shuffle). Easy: the words tagged `"difficulty": "easy"` first (when there are more than five, five of them at random, so in animals all six of duck, dog, cat, fish, bird and frog come up over the rounds), then the others shortest first (a stable sort over the shuffle, so words of the same length come at random); the five picked are shuffled again, so the round plays them in random order. Only the exact tag `easy` counts; a word with no tag is one of "the others", and a theme with no tags gets its shortest words.
- noted: transportation tags exactly five words easy (boat, bus, car, scooter, taxi), so every easy round of it is those five, including the 7-letter scooter, which comes before the 3-letter words that are not tagged; that is the acceptance's "prefers words tagged easy" read literally, and the content is read-only in M1.

- considered: playing the five shortest first within the round (the title's "short words first" read as an order; it would also make every easy round open the same way); the shortest five of the easy-tagged when there are more (dog and cat in every animals round, frog or bird never); counting "Easy" or other spellings (the acceptance names the exact tag).
- reversible: yes (one line: drop the last shuffle to play them shortest first).

## 2026-10-15 — Lever on easy: right after a wrong check only the wrong letters go back and the right ones lock; any other pull starts the word over except the given letter (B-205)
- chose: "after a wrong check" means `current.status === 'wrong'`: the checked letters have not moved since the check. That is the window in which DESIGN keeps the dome off "until a block moves", and state.js defines 'wrong' as "checked, lives left; the next block move makes it 'playing' again". In it, on easy (`levelRules(level).leverDropsWrongFirst`), a pull sends back the letters in wrong slots and locks the ones in right slots. Every other pull sends back every placed letter except the easy given letter, and nothing locks: before any check, after a block has moved since the check, and on normal and hard always. A lever-locked letter goes back too, and its slot is emptied, which unlocks it. A wrong-letters pull reopens the word ('playing', as any move does), so the pull after it starts the word over, whatever the kid did in between ("any time later"). A new wrong check (the second miss) makes the next pull a wrong-letters pull again. The letters an earlier pull locked are right, so they stay locked, and newly right letters lock with them. Right and wrong are judged per slot by letter, as the check does, so either of two same-letter blocks is right in that letter's slot. The given letter is known by its tray tile's `given` (B-204). It is in neither list and always stays.
- because: BACKLOG B-205; DECISIONS "M2 levels and hints" ("Lever on easy after a wrong check: first pull drops only the wrong letters and the right ones stay; pulling again starts the word over"). The helper's signature is `leverResult(level, current)`, and `current.status` is what it can see. Once a block has moved, the machine cannot tell right from wrong without a check of an arrangement the kid has not checked. Locking right letters then would be a free check that costs no bulb.
- considered: (a) "any wrong check on this word", even after moves. That gives a free check after rearranging, and `leverResult` would need `lives` or a new flag. (b) Remembering the checked slots in a new state field and dropping only those that still hold the checked block. That is a second record of placement to keep in step on every move, for a kid who is already fixing letters by hand. (c) Making the pull after a new wrong check a start-over because it is "the next pull". The human's rule reads per check, and the second miss would then lose the help.
- noted: issue #52 (unanswered; default: no doc changes) is not applied to any doc. Its suggestion B (one `locked` flag on placed slots, handled in drag.js) describes the code as it is, and B-205 locks only through that flag.
- reversible: yes (one condition in `leverResult`).

## 2026-10-15 — `leverResult` in drag.js beside the lock rules; `returnAll(current, level)` applies it; nothing new in card.js, CSS, feedback.js or character.js (B-205)
- chose: `leverResult(level, current)` is in `static/js/drag.js`, next to `isLocked`, `placeIn`, `takeOut` and `returnAll`. It is pure and reads `levelRules` from level.js. drag.js touches the DOM only inside its handlers, so node imports it, as the B-114 and B-204 tests already do. It returns `{ back, lock }`, the tile ids that go back to the bench and the ones that stay and lock, both in slot order. `returnAll(current, level)` keeps its name (the lever's transition, as #52 B and the batch instruction name it). It applies the result: a `back` slot becomes null and its tile `used: false`; a `lock` slot becomes `{ ...p, locked: true }`; the given letter is left as it is; the status goes through `afterMove`. main.js `onLever` passes `state.round.level`, never `state.level`. The lock is B-204's: card.js already sets `.letter-box.locked` (the amber edge) and keeps the block's Draggable disabled, and drag.js already refuses a drop on the slot, a drag, a tap-out and a swap. A lever-locked block is not `given`, so its bench spot stays empty while it is locked, and a start-over pull hops it back to where it lay. No new motion and no new sound: feedback.js `hopBack` already hops only blocks that left their slot (a letter left or locked stays still under the rail shake), and the robot plays `blockReturned` (idle, silent) as for any pull. Only comments change in state.js, round.js, feedback.js and game.css (no cache-buster bump: no rule changed).
- considered: putting `leverResult` in controls.js beside `canReset` and `speakerAction` (drag.js would then import controls.js, or `returnAll` would move away from the other lock rules); renaming `returnAll` to `pullLever` (churn against #52 B's wording); a new `lever.js` module (one function).
- tests: new `LeverTests` (9, run through node): easy right after a wrong check (back the wrong letters, lock the right ones; the locked letter refuses a drop, a move and a tap-out; the dome is off; the lever still works); the easy second pull (only the given letter stays; blocks placed after the first pull go back too; with only the given letter left, nothing goes back); a new wrong check after the first pull (wrong letters again, earlier and new right letters locked); a block moved since the wrong check (start over); normal and hard, wrong or not (everything back, nothing locks); before any check on every level (all but the given letter); a word with a letter twice (judged per slot by letter); the source wiring (`leverResult` and `returnAll` have no DOM or state and read `leverDropsWrongFirst`; `onLever` passes `state.round.level`). `ResetTransitionTests` now passes `'normal'` to `returnAll`, because B-114's lever is the normal-level behaviour. On the pre-B-205 frontend all 9 new tests fail. 192 tests in all, passing.
- checked (this branch, 360x740, headless Chromium, the real flow driven by clicks; a throwaway probe outside the repo): easy, scooter checked as "sctooer": the first pull sends t and o to the bench and locks c, o, e, r; a tap on a locked c does nothing; the dome is off; t and o tapped back in the same wrong slots and checked wrong again (one bulb left), then a pull sends only t and o back again; o and t into the right slots, then a check: correct, mastered with one bulb. No page errors.
- screenshots (`.agent/screenshots/B-205/`, tools/shot.mjs at 360x740, transportation, the first word retried until it is scooter, slots and bench asserted in `--eval`): `lever-easy-wrong.png` (easy, after the wrong check "sctooer", for context: one bulb out, the dome off), `lever-easy-after-wrong.png` (then the lever: t and o on the bench; s, c, o, e, r locked), `lever-easy-second-pull.png` (then the lever again: only the given s stays), `lever-normal-after-wrong.png` (normal, the same wrong check and one pull: every block on the bench, nothing locked), `lever-easy-before-check.png` (easy, "scoo" placed and no check, then the lever: only s stays, the right c, o, o do not lock).

## 2026-10-15 — Second miss: the hint is a pure transition applied with the wrong check; the block comes from the bench if one lies there, else from the wrong slot it is in (B-206)
- chose: `hintFor(current)` and `giveHint(current)` in `static/js/drag.js`, beside `leverResult` / `returnAll` and the lock rules; pure, no DOM, tested through node. `hintFor` returns `{ index, tileId, back }`: the first slot that is empty or holds a wrong letter (a locked slot is always right and is skipped), a block with that slot's letter, and the block already in that slot (null when it is empty). The block is one with that letter lying on the bench (`used: false`) if there is one, else the one in the first wrong slot holding that letter, which is emptied; a block in a right slot is never taken, so the given letter and lever-locked letters are never moved. `giveHint` puts it in the slot as `{ letter, tileId, locked: true, hint: true }` and sends the old occupant back to its bench spot (`used: false`); it keeps the status. main.js `onCheck` applies it in the same setState as the wrong check when the miss leaves one bulb (`left === 1`), on every level (it reads no level), so the word stays 'wrong' and the dome stays off until a block moves (a slot is empty after the hint anyway). The lock is B-204's flag (`locked: true`: card.js draws the amber edge and keeps the block's Draggable disabled; drag.js refuses a drop, a drag, a tap-out and a swap); `hint: true` only says whose letter it is (the lever and the robot key on it). Nothing else changes for "mastered": onCheck's correct branch compares the letters with the word and adds the sticker before it looks at lives, so a word checked correct after the hint (one bulb lit) is mastered, and the reveal keeps a right slot's object (`{ ...p }`), so the hint letter stays locked through a third miss.
- because: BACKLOG B-206 ("the wrong check that leaves one bulb lit also places one letter: the first slot that is empty or wrong gets its correct letter, flown in from the bench like the reveal fly-in (a wrong letter in that slot goes back to the bench first). That letter locks."); the batch instruction (lock through the same `locked` flag, reuse the look); CLAUDE.md (state changes only through setState, pure transitions tested through node, feedback.js owns the fly-in). At a check every slot is filled, and on easy and normal every block is then in a slot, so the right block is always in another (wrong) slot: "from the bench" is read as "from where the block lies", the bench when one is there (on hard with B-207's extras it can be). "Empty" cannot happen at a check on easy and normal; the helper handles it anyway, so it is right for any state. Doing it in the check's own setState keeps state and screen in one transition: nothing moves under a finger later, and the robot and the motion see the miss and the hint together.
- considered: (a) a second setState after the robot's confused reaction, from a timer in main.js (it would move blocks the kid may already be dragging, and needs a word guard; "empty" would then be reachable); (b) sending every wrong letter back to the bench first, then flying the hint letter from the bench (more help than "one letter", and on normal and hard it would give easy's wrong-letters-first lever for free); (c) swapping the displaced wrong letter into the hint block's old slot (that places two letters); (d) the hint marked on the tray tile like `given` (the lock and the hint belong to the slot, as B-204 decided for locks; a tray flag would also need card.js not to treat it as `given`).
- note for B-207: on hard, when the only wrong slot holds an extra letter and the right block lies on the bench, the hint completes the word while the status stays 'wrong', so the dome stays off until a block moves. B-207 may want the hint to reopen the word in that case.
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-15 — The robot's `hintGiven`: happy with a 10px pop, no clip, starting when his peek at the miss is over (B-206)
- chose: a new row in character.js `MOMENTS`: `hintGiven: { steps: [{ pose: 'happy', move: 'smallPop' }], sound: null }`, and a new move `smallPop`: the pop at half the height (rises 10px in 180ms, ease-out, settles in 120ms). `snapshot` gains `hint` (the hint slot's index, or -1; `NONE.hint` is -1), and `momentsFor` returns `['wrongPeek', 'hintGiven']` when a wrong check also brought a hint slot (`['wrong']` / `['wrongPeek']` as before otherwise); a re-render, a later move and the reveal after it do not replay it. Timing: `stepDelays(count, sinceLast, lasts)` gains an optional third argument, how long each step's move lasts (new `moveMs(kind, reduced)`); the next step starts CHANGE_MS after a step or when its move ends, whichever is later. renderRobot passes it. The peek lasts 950ms (150ms down, 600ms of only his eyes, 200ms up), so `hintGiven` starts at 950ms, as the peek ends; under reduced motion moves take 0ms and it comes at 300ms. Every other run of steps (kidIdle, thirdMiss, roundStart + picture) moves for 300ms or less, so their delays are unchanged (a test checks this). His pop rises less than the 20px pop, so it stays clear of the blocks, as CLAUDE.md's robot rule wants.
- because: BACKLOG B-206 ("a new moment `hintGiven` (happy pose, small pop) after his confused reaction to the miss, per CHARACTER.md 'Presence' spacing"). On the miss that leaves one bulb, his confused reaction is the peek, which CHARACTER "Presence" defines as "only eyes above the edge for 600ms, then normal"; starting the next step at 300ms would cut it short, so the step waits for it. "Small pop" is read as a smaller version of the correct answer's pop: the hint is help, not a win. No clip: the miss already plays its error clip in the same render, M1 has no "here is a letter" line, and DESIGN "Sound" gives the reveal fly-in no clip of its own (the error clip only), so the hint's fly-in follows it (no swipe either).
- considered: `hintGiven` 300ms after the confused step, by the existing CHANGE_MS spacing (cuts the peek's 600ms); the 20px `pop` (the same as a correct answer); a "correct" or swipe clip on the landing (audio.js plays a moment's clip at once, so it would sound before the letter moves, and a voice clip would cut his error clip).
- noted: a block moved before 950ms is a new moment and, by the existing rule in renderRobot ("a new moment replaces the steps an earlier one still had waiting"), replaces the waiting `hintGiven`, as it already cuts the peek. The letter still flies in (feedback.js does not depend on the robot).
- not edited (docs change only with the human's approval): suggested CHARACTER.md "Moment map" row after "Wrong check": "Second miss: his letter (B-206) | happy, small pop (10px), after the peek | — | always".

## 2026-10-16 — Hard's extra letters: a confusion table and `extraLetters` in round.js; `makeWord` marks their blocks `extra` (B-207)
- chose: `static/js/round.js` gains `CONFUSIONS` (letter pairs) and `extraLetters(word, count, rand)`: `count` different letters that are not in the word, first the partners of the word's letters (a letter paired with one in the word and not itself in it), at random, then random letters a–z not in the word. `makeWord` asks for `levelRules(level).distractors` of them (2 on hard, 0 on easy and normal, B-202's table) and shuffles their blocks in with the word's, each marked `extra: true` on its tray tile (a flag like `given`). With 0, `extraLetters` returns [] without calling `rand`, and the shuffle sees as many blocks as before, so easy and normal words come out exactly as before (same order, same tray objects; a test compares them with the old shuffle). card.js is unchanged: every tray tile that is not `given` gets a bench spot, so the hard bench holds n + 2 blocks. An extra looks like any other block (no mark: the kid has to choose).
- chose (the table): the eight pairs the backlog names (b/d, p/q, c/k, s/z, m/n, i/e, a/e, u/o), plus v/w (one sound for Hebrew speakers, the classic mix-up), c/s (the soft c of "bicycle") and i/y (the vowel y of "bicycle"). A letter can have more than one partner (c: k, s; e: i, a; i: e, y). Examples: duck gets two of b, o, s; helicopter two of a, y, k, s, u, q; wolf gets u and v.
- because: BACKLOG B-207 ("2 extra letters that are not in the word, picked from a confusion table in a pure helper (pairs such as ...): prefer the partner of a letter in the word, else a random letter not in the word. Never a letter already in the word"); CLAUDE.md: round.js sets up a word's blocks, pure and tested through node; rules from `levelRules(state.round.level)`.
- considered: the extras as a list beside the tray (card.js, drag.js and feedback.js all key on tray tiles, so they would each need a second path); only the eight named pairs ("such as" leaves room, and v/w matters most for these players); the same wrong letter twice (a weaker choice for the kid); weighting partners by how often their letter occurs in the word; a visible mark on the extras (it would give the answer away).

Before this, the reveal marked every block `used`, so an extra in a slot would have stayed in that slot's DOM beside the right letter (card.js mounts a block without removing the one there).

## 2026-10-16 — The robot's letter can finish the word on hard: the word reopens and the dome lights (B-207; B-206's note, #54 A)
- chose: handle it. New pure `afterHint(current)` in drag.js: a 'wrong' word whose every slot holds its right letter becomes 'playing'; anything else is returned as it is. main.js onCheck calls it right after the miss's setState (which still carries 'wrong' and the hint) and applies a change in a setState of its own. The first setState is what the robot (`wrongPeek`, `hintGiven`), the error clip and feedback.js (the bulb, the static, the wiggle, the hop and the fly-in) react to; the second changes only the status, so it is no moment, no sound and no motion (the placement is the same), and the dome goes `ready`. The kid presses it, and a correct check is mastered with one bulb, as after any hint. This happens only on hard: on easy and normal every block is in a slot at a check, so the hint always leaves a slot empty. When the hint leaves another wrong letter (a second extra in a slot, or letters in the wrong order), nothing changes: 'wrong', and the dome stays off until a block moves.

Here the robot moved a block and the arrangement is new and complete; without this, the finished word sits under a dark dome until the kid takes a letter out and puts it back. It is also what the B-206 reviewer's #54 A proposes ("the word reopens: status 'playing', dome ready, no block has to move first"). #54 is unanswered; its default (skip) only means no BACKLOG line is added, and the batch instruction left this to the implementer (ask-rule: decided and logged, no question).

- noted: the dome is ready while his letter is still flying in (it lands at 900ms); a press then checks at once, since the motion is not state.
- reversible: yes (the two lines after the miss's setState in onCheck).

The test now expects d from slot 2 at 0s and u from its bench spot at 0.12s at the bench's size, and the b to hop back; on the round-0 feedback.js it fails.
- chose (2): the five-block first row is not "at 360" only. `blockLayout(12, w)` gives 5 + 4 + 3 for every bench from 324px to 433px, that is every play column narrower than 462px (the 360, 375, 390, 412 and 430 phones). From 434px of bench (a 462px column, in practice the 480px desktop column) it gives 4 + 4 + 4. The layout.js comments now say so. The round-0 entry's "at 360" wording for the bend stands corrected here; the bend itself is unchanged. Corrected suggestion, not edited (docs change only with the human's approval): DESIGN.md "Layout", Letter blocks: "... in loose staggered rows (max 4 per row; on hard a 10-letter word's 12 blocks put five on the first row in a play column narrower than 462px, and a third row of three starts right of the robot's reach, B-207)."

- chose (4): a tap on a bench block while every slot is full stays a no-op, intended. On hard two blocks are always left on the bench when the rail is full, so this is new with B-207. With a real touch, the block lifts and settles (the pick-up and put-down, 120ms each) and nothing else happens: no state change, no sound, no robot moment. Checked in the browser: state unchanged, the block back in its spot, no page error. Why: a full rail has no free slot, and the tap's rule ("the first empty slot") has nowhere to put it. Choosing a slot for the kid would be arbitrary (the last one) or would give the answer away (a wrong one), and choosing letters is what hard is for. The kid can drag the block onto a slot, which swaps it (the old letter goes back to the bench, `placeIn`), or tap a placed letter back out first. The lift-and-settle already acknowledges the touch, as for a tap on a locked letter (B-204, also a no-op). The spring-back is the same thing here, since a tap does not move the block. Considered: a "no" wiggle on the block (DESIGN uses the wiggle for a wrong check); swapping it into the last slot.
- chose (5): re-shot `hard-hint-finishes.png` and `hard-hint-then-correct.png` as one run. Both `--eval`s seed `Math.random` the same way (a screenshot-only override; the app is unchanged) and assert the same word and bench ("duck", tray o* k d b* c u, the extras o and b), so the second is the first plus a press on the dome. The round-0 entry's caption for `hard-hint-then-correct.png` described a continuation, but that shot was a separate run (its extras were b and o, the other's o and s). `hard-revealed.png` was re-shot from the same seed (o and b in slots 1-2, his d after the second miss, then the reveal: u in, both extras back on the bench), and `hard-revealed-flying.png` was added: the same run frozen (`gsap.globalTimeline.pause()` in the `--eval`) about 55ms into the reveal, with the u flying up from its bench spot and the b hopping out of slot 2.

## 2026-10-16 — Missed words: a new `missed.js` owns `flip.missed.v1`; a round is recorded when it ends; "skipped" is a word left without a correct check; a round left with the back button records nothing (B-208)
- chose: a new `static/js/missed.js`, no DOM and no module state, built like stickers.js, is the only module that names localStorage `flip.missed.v1`. Shape `{ "<theme>": ["rabbit", ...] }`, the oldest miss first, each word at most once per theme. It exports `load()` (sanitised: bad JSON, a non-object, a theme whose value is not an array, non-string or empty entries and duplicates are dropped, and an empty list is dropped; never throws, and `localStorage` itself is read inside a try, since reading it throws when the browser blocks storage), `wordsFor(theme)` (that theme's list, or []), the pure `afterRound(words, results)` and `record(theme, results)`. Going through a round's results in order, `afterRound` takes each word out of the list, and a word not checked correct goes back in at the end as the newest miss. Words the round did not play keep their place. `record` writes nothing when the list does not change and drops a theme whose list is left empty. main.js calls `record(state.theme, results)` once, in `onNext`'s round-end branch, before the setState that shows the round-end screen. `startRound` reads `wordsFor(name)` for the pick (next entry). Nothing changes on the screen, in state.js, card.js, character.js, audio.js or the CSS: a word that comes back looks and plays like any other.
- chose ("revealed or skipped"): today a word ends only with a correct check or the reveal. B-114 removed the Next button ("no skipping a word"), and `onNext` records a word with no result as `{ correct: false }`, defensively. So a missed word is any round result not checked correct: the revealed words, plus any word that still reaches the round end without a result. If a later item adds a skip that records `correct: false`, it is covered as it stands. A word checked correct is removed, which is always with at least one bulb left (the third miss reveals).
- chose (when): the list is written at the round end, not when a word is revealed or checked. A round left with the back button never reaches that point, so it changes nothing: a word revealed in it is not remembered, and a remembered word spelled in it stays remembered. Checked in the browser (normal, animals, the list seeded `{animals: [horse]}`): bear, rabbit and duck were revealed, horse was spelled, then the back arrow was pressed. The list was still `{animals: [horse]}`.
- because: BACKLOG B-208 ("remembered per theme in localStorage (`flip.missed.v1`, only read/written in one module, like stickers.js)"; "a word spelled correctly is removed from the list"). B-201 drops an unfinished round with "no stars, no stickers, no results kept" and takes its new stickers back out of the book. Writing at the round end gives the same rule for the missed list without any undo step. CLAUDE.md asks for one module per localStorage key and for pure logic tested through node.
- considered: (a) writing on the reveal and on a correct check, as stickers.js is written in `onCheck`. A dropped round would then need its writes undone, as B-201 does for stickers, and the list would need a record of what it held before the round. (b) Keeping the misses of a dropped round, since the kid did struggle with them. That goes against B-201's "no results kept", and B-201 already treats a sticker that way. (c) The word on screen when the kid leaves counted as "skipped". B-201 makes leaving a plain exit with no result. (d) A cap on the list. None is needed: each word is in it at most once, so a theme's list is no longer than the theme (12 and 14 words in M1).
- noted: a word that a theme no longer has (content is read-only in M1, so only a hand-edited store) stays in the list. The pick skips it (next entry), and it comes back if the word comes back.
- not edited (docs change only with the human's approval): suggested CLAUDE.md "Frontend architecture" line after `level.js`: "`missed.js` is the only place localStorage `flip.missed.v1` (the missed words per theme, `{ "<theme>": [word, ...] }`, the oldest miss first) is read or written (`load`/`wordsFor`/`record`); no DOM, no module state. main.js reads `wordsFor(theme)` in `startRound` and calls `record(theme, results)` only at a round's end (`onNext`), so a round left with the back button records nothing; `afterRound(words, results)` is the pure part; a Django test runs it through `node`."
- reversible: yes (the store is new; one call in `onNext`).

## 2026-10-16 — The pick: the two oldest remembered words the theme still has, on every level; the other places by the level's pick from the words not remembered; all in random places (B-208)
- chose: new pure `pickRound(data, size, level, missed, rand)` in round.js, beside `pickWords`, which is unchanged. It takes the first `MISSED_BACK` (2) words of `missed` that the theme's data still has, ignoring duplicates. It fills the other places with `pickWords` (the level's pick: easy's tagged-then-shortest, or normal's and hard's random pick) from the theme's words that are not remembered. Only when those run out does it take the other remembered words. Then it shuffles all of them together, so the remembered words land anywhere in the round. With no remembered word that the theme has, it returns `pickWords` exactly, with the same random numbers (a test checks this per level), so a kid with no misses gets the rounds of before. main.js `startRound` calls `pickRound(data, ROUND_SIZE, level, missedWords(name))` in place of `pickWords(data, ROUND_SIZE, level)`.
- chose (which two): the oldest. A word missed again goes to the end of the list (previous entry), so every remembered word comes back in turn. Example: misses a, b, c, d make the next round bring a and b. If both are missed again, the list is c, d, a, b, and the round after brings c and d.
- chose ("up to 2" as a cap): a round holds no more than two remembered words while the theme has enough others. The other places come from the words that are not remembered, so a kid with many misses does not get a round made only of words they could not spell. In M1 content the fallback to the other remembered words is reached only when fewer than three words of the theme are not remembered.
- chose (levels): the list is per theme, as the acceptance says, so remembered words come back on every level. On easy that includes a word easy would not pick, such as a long word missed on hard (helicopter). It then gets easy's help: the first letter given and the word on the speaker at any time. The other places follow the level: on easy, the easy-tagged words first, then the shortest of the words not remembered.
- because: BACKLOG B-208 ("The next round of that theme includes up to 2 remembered missed words, in random positions"; "A Django test runs the round-picking helper through node"); BACKLOG B-204 (easy's pick for the other places); CLAUDE.md (round.js sets up a round, is pure and is tested through node). A separate function keeps `pickWords` and its B-204 tests as they are.
- considered: (a) picking the two at random from the list. That is just as fair over many rounds, but the oldest-first order is predictable and can be tested without luck. (b) Letting the other places fall on remembered words by chance, which can put 3 to 5 hard words in one round. (c) Keeping the list per level, or bringing back on easy only the words easy would pick. The acceptance says per theme, and a hard word practised with easy's help is still practice. (d) A `missed` parameter on `pickWords` itself. Its random-number argument comes fourth, so every existing call and test would change.
- tests: new `MissedWordsTests` (21). Through node with a fake localStorage and a seeded random: missed.js (missing key; the documented shape after a round end; a word without `correct` or with `correct: false` is missed; a correct word comes out and a re-miss goes to the end while other themes stay; the last word out drops the theme; no write when nothing changes or there is no theme; corrupt JSON and bad entries; no storage or blocked storage; `afterRound` pure). `pickRound` (with no remembered word it equals `pickWords` on every level; the two oldest come back on every level and never a third; with one remembered word, easy fills from its tagged words; on transportation easy, helicopter and submarine come back with three of the five tagged words; normal and hard fill from the whole theme; random places, every position seen; words the theme does not have are skipped and duplicates ignored; a theme with every word remembered still gets five; size 1 takes the oldest; inputs untouched; three rounds through both modules as main.js runs them). Source wiring: only missed.js names `flip.missed`, and it has no DOM or imports; `pickRound` calls `pickWords` and has no state or storage; main.js imports missed.js, picks with `missedWords(name)`, and calls `recordMissed` once, in `onNext`'s round-end branch before the screen changes, and not in onPlayBack, onThemes, onCheck or startRound; stickers are still added only in onCheck's correct branch. Adapted: `EasyStartTests.test_main_builds_the_round_from_the_rounds_level` now expects `pickRound` and its call. On the pre-B-208 frontend, 20 of the 21 new tests fail, along with the adapted one. The one that passes guards the unchanged sticker rule. 245 tests in all, passing.

Each `--eval` plays real rounds with clicks on the level button, the theme card, the blocks and the dome: a reveal is three wrong checks, with the second-miss hint. `Math.random` is seeded in the `--eval` so the runs replay the same words (a screenshot-only override; the app is unchanged), and every `--eval` checks its words and the stored list and throws if they differ. `round-end-missed.png`: round 1 on normal, animals (horse, rabbit, bird, wolf, frog), with rabbit and wolf revealed; 3 / 5, both bulbs dark; the list is now `{animals: [rabbit, wolf]}`. `play-missed-back.png`: the round after it (the play-again button: frog, tiger, bear, wolf, rabbit), word 4 / 5 is wolf, a remembered word. `play-missed-back-2.png`: the same round, word 5 / 5 is rabbit, the other remembered word. `round-end-missed-back.png`: that round's end, with wolf spelled (lit, new sticker) and rabbit missed again (dark, no sticker); the list is now `{animals: [rabbit]}`, and rabbit is not in the sticker book. `play-easy-missed-back.png`: easy, transportation, the list seeded `{transportation: [helicopter]}`; the round is boat, taxi, bus, scooter and helicopter, and at 5 / 5 helicopter starts with its h given and locked.

## 2026-10-16 — Level-up streak: `state.streak` for the session, moved once per finished round; fewer stars or another level start it over; the back button neither breaks nor counts (B-209)
- chose: state.js gets `streak: { level, rounds }` (default `{ level: null, rounds: 0 }`): the rounds in a row finished with 3 stars on one level, this session only. It is not stored (the acceptance says "not stored across page loads"), so a reload starts at none. The rules are pure, in level.js beside `levelRules` (no DOM, no module state, run through node): `nextLevel(level)` (easy → normal → hard → null; anything that is not a level → null), `streakAfter(streak, level, stars)` and `levelUpFor(streak)`, with `LEVEL_UP_ROUNDS = 2`. main.js writes the streak in one place: `onNext`'s round-end branch, in the setState that shows the round-end screen, as `streakAfter(state.streak, round.level, stars)` with `stars` from score.js `starsFor`, the same count the screen shows. The round-end render shows the nudge when `levelUpFor(state.streak)` gives a level.
- chose (what breaks it): a round with fewer than 3 stars leaves `{ level, rounds: 0 }`. A round on another level starts a new streak on that level: 1 with 3 stars, else 0. So "two in a row on the same level" means the last two finished rounds. The theme does not matter: a streak runs across themes, since the level is what the nudge is about. Picking a level on the theme screen changes nothing by itself; the next finished round's level decides.
- chose (the back button): a round left with the back arrow neither breaks the streak nor adds to it. It never reaches `onNext`'s round-end branch, so nothing is written. This is the rule B-201 gives a dropped round ("no stars, no stickers, no results kept") and B-208 follows for the missed words. Checked in the browser: easy, one 3-star round (streak 1), then two words of the next round and the back arrow (still 1), then a theme and a 3-star round: streak 2, and the nudge shows.
- chose (repeats): the nudge shows whenever the streak is 2 or more. A kid who passes it by and keeps getting 3 stars on the same level sees it again after each such round, since the last two rounds are again "two in a row". It is one glowing button beside play again, never a block, so this stays "never forced". On hard `nextLevel` is null: no nudge, whatever the streak.
- chose (after the tap): the streak is not reset when the kid takes the nudge. The next round is on the new level, so its end starts a new streak there.
- because: BACKLOG B-209 and the orchestrator's note ("session-only, so it belongs in state.js"); CLAUDE.md (state only through setState; level rules in level.js; pure helpers tested through node; `levelRules(state.round.level)`, so the round's level, not the picker's).
- considered: (a) the back button breaking the streak (a kid leaving a round may be finding it hard, but B-201 makes leaving a plain exit with no result, and B-208 records nothing for it); (b) resetting the streak once the nudge has shown, so it comes every two rounds (fewer repeats, but after a third 3-star round in a row the acceptance's condition holds again); (c) a streak per theme (the nudge is about the level, and the acceptance names only the level); (d) counting stars inside level.js from the results (score.js already owns stars, and main.js computes them for the screen the same way); (e) the stars or the streak in localStorage (not stored, per the acceptance).
- reversible: yes (one state field, one line in `onNext`).

- chose (1, blocking): `#screen-round-end .actions { margin-bottom: 10px; }`, on every round-end, not only when the nudge shows. It sets it true right after reading the level and before fetching the theme's data file. It sets it false in the setState that shows the round, or right away if the fetch fails, so the next tap can try again. `onLevelUp` also does nothing while it is true, so a nudge tapped just after play again cannot change the level under the round play again started. Only `startRound` writes it. The round-0 code let a second quick tap on the nudge, on play again or on a theme card start a second round (the screen stays where it is until the fetch resolves). `roundsStarted` then went up by 2, which cut the robot's "first 2 rounds" dome-pointing short. Checked in the browser with two `click()`s in a row: a theme card, play again and the nudge each start one round (round-0 code: two each). ## 2026-10-17 — The puppet in `character.js`: the prototype's tables, moments as timed steps, one module (B-210)
- chose: `static/js/character.js` keeps the whole robot, as before: above "Render" the pure part, below it the DOM/GSAP part. The pure part now holds the prototype's numbers (`design/reference/robot-puppet.html`) as tables: `HANDS` (its `H`), `REST`, `EYES`, `BROWS`, `PIVOT` (head, body, eyebrows), `HAND_SHAPES` (file per side, width in puppet px), `TIMING` (each part's tween and the pop, sink and shake), `AMBIENT` (float, breathing, hand drift, eyebrow bob, blink, wander, eyebrow solo), `FX` (flourishes: giggle, wag, browJump, shiver, pump, pump2, clap), `PEEK`, `AIM` and `TALK`. `MOMENTS` keeps its keys and sound column (audio.js is unchanged there); each moment is now a list of steps `{ at, sink?, head?, eyes?, brows?, hands?, look?, pop?, shake?, fx?, peek? }` at the reference's times (its `run()` waits added up), or `variants` with a `chance` each (picture: surprise / giggle / shock, a third each; filledEarly: point 0.6 / excited 0.4). New pure helpers: `variantsOf`, `pickVariant(name, r)`, `stepsOf`, `isChange`, `scriptFor(moments, picks)`, `startDelay`, `reducedStep`, `fxFor`, `handPose`, `eyeShape`, `browPose`, `pct`, `aimAt`, and the talking helpers (next entries). Gone: the sprite `POSES`, `MOVES`, `moveFor`, `moveMs`, `stepsFor`, `stepDelays`, `ROBOT_H`. The render part: `renderRobot` (subscriber, as before), `playMoments(moments, picks)`, `followBlock(point)` and `renderTalk(wave)`; internally the reference's building blocks (hands, eyes, brows, head, look, pop, sink, shake, flourish) and the ambient loops. His pose lives on `.robot` as classes (`eyes-<name>`, `brows-<name>`, `hands-<name>`, `is-sunk`, `is-following`) and the head's turn in `data-head`; it is read back only by the render (no blink on happy or squinting eyes, no wander while pointing or following, no eyebrow solo while sunk, the shake and the drag return to the head's pose), never by game logic.
- chose (timing): a state change's moments (at most two: round start + picture; a miss + hintGiven) become one script: the next moment starts when the one before takes its last step (at least CHANGE_MS after it started), and that last step is dropped, since every moment's first step sets a whole pose. A new moment replaces any steps still waiting (and the idle peeking), its first step no sooner than 300ms after his last pose change (CHARACTER "Presence"). Within the prototype's moments the steps that change his pose are already at least 300ms apart (glances and flourishes come between; a test checks it). A variant is picked with `Math.random()` when the moment plays: render-side randomness, not state, nothing reads it.
- because: the acceptance puts the moments in `character.js`'s `MOMENTS` and the prototype's numbers are the spec; tables of data can be compared with the prototype itself in a test (`PuppetTests` reads its `H`, `EYES`, `BROWS`, `REST` and checks each other number next to the prototype line it comes from). One module keeps CLAUDE.md's "character.js is the only place the robot's poses and moves are decided" true without a second module that would import character.js and be imported back by it.
- considered: a separate `puppet.js` for the DOM part (character.js would import it to play steps and it would import character.js's tables: a module cycle); keeping the pose in a module-level render cache (CLAUDE.md puts his current pose on the `.robot` class); a GSAP timeline handle per moment (a module global; `killTweensOf(applyStep)` / `killTweensOf(peekOnce)` drop waiting steps without one, as B-115 did).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-17 — Canvas-sized layers moved in % of the canvas; the scale stays CSS (B-210)
- chose: every moving part (`.pup-in`, `.pup-float`, the body, the head, each eyebrow and its bob layer, `.hands-in`, `.hands-float`, each hand, its drift and its flip layer) is a layer the size of the 505 canvas (`.part`, `inset: 0`), so GSAP moves it with `xPercent` / `yPercent` (`pct(px)` = px / 505 * 100) and turns it about a point given in % of the canvas (`transform-origin: calc(100% * 241 / 505) ...`). The mittens sit in their hand layer at the rest point (`--hx`, `--hy`) and are sized in % of it; the visor's SVG is the canvas (viewBox 1264), so the eyes, scope and sparks move in its units as in the prototype. No JS reads a size and nothing re-renders on resize.
- because: the prototype scales a 505px box with `transform: scale(k)`; a unitless k from the console's width needs a length divided by a length in CSS, which Safari 16.2 / Chrome 111 cannot do (CLAUDE.md browser floor), so it would have to be measured and set from JS on every resize.
- considered: a JS-set `--k` scale on resize (a layout read in the render and a resize path); converting every number to CSS px in JS (the tables would no longer read like the prototype).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-17 — Talking: an AnalyserNode on his voice in audio.js, its samples handed to character.js every frame by main.js; the target word stays out (B-210)
- chose: audio.js makes one `AnalyserNode` (FFT 1024, the prototype's) with the AudioContext on the first gesture and connects it to the output; the robot's clips (the `voice` channel: correct, error, tada, locked) play into it, the swipe (`fx`) straight out. New export `voiceWave()`: the analyser's 512 time-domain samples while one of his clips plays, else null (also when muted: nothing plays; and without Web Audio or `createAnalyser`, when his clips go straight out). The analyser and the 512-byte array are engine handles kept beside `ctx`, `bytes`, `buffers` and `live`, read by no other module. main.js: `gsap.ticker.add(() => renderTalk(voiceWave()))`. character.js `renderTalk` smooths the loudness and draws the four effects through pure helpers (`loudness`, `smoothLevel`, `browRide`, `scopeLine`, `talkLights`, `nodDue`, constants in `TALK`); its render cache `talk` (level, eyebrow lift, last bob) only carries one frame's drawing to the next, and once he is quiet and drawn so it does nothing. Off the play screen it draws nothing. The head bob is off under reduced motion; the scope, eye pulse, cap glow and eyebrows are light and shape and stay.
- chose (the target word): not routed through the analyser. It stays an `<audio>` element played straight out (no `MediaElementSource`), so the robot's mouth does not move with it.
- chose (cap flash): the 3-in-a-row spark flash is a second copy of the cap's glow and sparks (`.cap-flash`), tweened on its own. In the prototype both use one element: the flash leaves its CSS opacity at 0, which then hides the talking glow (an attribute) for good.
- because: DESIGN "Sound" and CHARACTER "Rules" keep "voice clip" (his clips, one at a time) apart from the target word, and audio.js is the only place sounds play. A `MediaElementSource` would move the word into the AudioContext: silent while the context is suspended or interrupted (an app switch on iOS) and under iOS 16's silent switch, where today the word plays as a media element; the word is the one sound that must always be heard. The ticker hand-off avoids a module cycle (audio.js already imports character.js, so character.js cannot import audio.js: audio.js's top level uses `NONE` from it) and keeps a frame loop out of audio.js.
- considered: character.js pulling `voiceWave` itself (the cycle above); audio.js running a requestAnimationFrame loop and calling the robot (sound code driving visuals); decoding each word for an offline analysis synced to `currentTime` (an extra fetch and decode per word, and no AnalyserNode); the level or "talking" in state (it changes every frame and nothing but the drawing reads it).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-17 — Eyes and head follow a dragged block: drag.js hands its centre to `character.js followBlock` (B-210)
- chose: drag.js's Draggable gets `onDrag() { followBlock(centre(this.target)); }` and `followBlock(null)` in `onRelease`. `followBlock` (character.js) aims the eyes and turns the head from his head's point on the canvas (the prototype's 53% / 37%) to the block's centre with the pure `aimAt(dx, dy)` (the prototype's numbers: eyes up to 30 / 18 visor units, head dx / 25 degrees, at most 8), marks `.robot` `is-following` (the wander waits), and on release looks ahead and turns the head back to its pose. Nothing is stored: the point is passed on and dropped. The drop's own moment (placed or returned) follows as before.
- because: drag.js already owns the drag and knows the block; the point is transient render input, not game state (it changes on every pointer move and decides nothing), so it never goes through setState. A direct call is the smallest route and keeps drag.js's game logic untouched.
- considered: the pointer or the dragged tile in state (a setState per pointer move for a render input); character.js listening to pointer moves itself (it would have to read drag.js's `gsap-dragging` class to know a block is being dragged); a custom DOM event (the same coupling, less visible).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-17 — Correction: the target word could be analysed without leaving its `<audio>` element; not moving his mouth with it is a product choice, left as built (B-210, review fix round 1)
- corrects: the "Talking" entry above. It said analysing the word would need a `MediaElementSource` (which would move the audible word into the AudioContext) or an offline analysis synced to `currentTime`. That is not the only way: a silent parallel path exists. The word's bytes can be decoded once more and played as an `AudioBufferSource` → the AnalyserNode → a gain node at 0, started together with the `<audio>` element. The kid still hears only the element, so the word plays however the context is (suspended, interrupted, iOS 16's silent switch), and his mouth could move with it.

This is a product choice (whether the robot visibly "says" the target word), not a technical limit. The reading behind it, that "a voice clip" in the acceptance means his clips and not the target word (DESIGN "Sound" keeps the two apart), stays recorded here only; CHARACTER.md "Poses" now says, as the acceptance does, "whenever a voice clip plays through `audio.js`", and its added sentence about the word is removed. The orchestrator takes the question to the human.
- considered (if the word should move his mouth): the silent parallel path above (one more fetch and decode per word, two playbacks to start together, drift between them only visual); a `MediaElementSource` (the audible word inside the context, with the risks above).
- reversible: yes.

- corrects: the "Two layers in the console's z-order" entry above. Its claim that the hands never come over a control's tap area was measured for the hand poses at rest only. In the first build a sink tweened the hands' layer down and its opacity to 0 together (the prototype's way), so on the idle sink and the second miss's peek, and again on the way back up, the half-transparent mittens slid across the console's plate through the lever's (x 37-85) and the speaker's (x 111-159) tap areas.
- chose: a pure `handsSink(px, reduced)` in character.js plans the hands' layer for a sink to `px`: below `TIMING.sink.hideHands` (40 puppet px) they vanish at once (`set` opacity 0) and then slide down hidden; back up to 40 or less they slide up hidden (0.3s) and then fade in (0.3s, after the slide). `sink()` first drops an opacity tween still waiting from the last sink, then applies the plan. Reduced motion: the same order, instant. So on the wake and on the return from the peek his hands come in a beat after he is up (from 0.3s, whole at 0.6s).
- tests: `PuppetTests.test_the_hands_hide_before_they_sink_and_show_after_they_rise` (the plan); `PuppetRenderTests.test_the_hands_vanish_before_he_sinks_and_return_after_he_rises` (the tweens in that order on the idle sink and the wake, the waiting fade dropped first, reduced motion); `RobotLayoutTests.test_he_fits_beside_the_bench_and_his_hands_clear_the_controls` now also measures how far down the resting hands can be and still clear every tap area (between 10 and 40 puppet px; the pop's 10px trail is within it), and simulates every sink a moment or the peek uses (0, 104, 150), from each to each, normal and reduced, every 5ms: wherever the hands are visible they are no lower than that. With the first build's plan these fail.

## 2026-10-17 — The clunk: feedback.js presses a letter in when it locks, its look switching at the hit; no sound (B-211)
- chose: a new pure `newlyLocked(prevPlaced, placed)` in feedback.js returns the slots that are locked now and were not at the last render (or held another block). renderFeedback runs it on every transition of the same word after its other checks: the slots that are not `hint` clunk at once (in practice the right letters the easy lever keeps, together with the rail shake and the hop-back of the wrong ones); the robot's letter clunks in `hintIn` as it lands (0.9s), after `travel`, which stops the block's tweens when it starts. Easy's given letter is locked from the word's first render, which renderFeedback never reacts to, so it never clunks. `clunk(el, at)`: the block gets class `landing` (drawn as a placed block, full slot size), is pressed to scale 0.8 in 90ms (`power2.in`, the hit); at the hit a delayed call removes `landing` (the brass plate comes on, 86% of the slot), measures the two sizes and goes on from the same on-screen size (0.8 x 46 / 40 = 0.92) up to 1 in 110ms (`power2.out`). 200ms in all, inside DESIGN "Motion"'s 120–300ms, no back.out overshoot. It ends at scale 1, the CSS render, so nothing is left to clear. A new spec in motion.js, `clunk: { duration: 0.2, ease: 'power2.out', scale: 0.8, press: 0.09, fade: 0.2 }`. Reduced motion: no press and no `landing`; the plate is on at once and the letter dips in opacity (0.4 to 1, 200ms, the wiggle's `dip`); the robot's letter, which already fades in under reduced motion, gets no second fade. The rebound tween is `lazy: false`. A probe sampling every GSAP tick found that a `fromTo` created inside the delayed call was drawn lazily on the next tick, so for one frame at the hit three of four letters showed 0.8 instead of 0.92. With `lazy: false` every letter goes 0.843, 0.92, 0.95.
- because: BACKLOG B-211 ("a short clunk moment (small press-in motion)" when a letter becomes locked, lever or hint); CLAUDE.md (feedback.js is the only place one-shot motion keyed on a state transition starts; it keeps only its render cache). Switching the look at the hit, not at the pull, makes the change of material part of the clunk: the cream block is pushed in and comes up as the machine's brass.
- sound: none. B-211 allows a sound "only if a fitting one exists". `static/sounds/` has the voice clips (correct, error, celebration, "hmm no") and the `swipe` effects, which DESIGN "Sound" keeps for a block going into a slot. None is a mechanical clunk, a swipe on a letter that does not move would say "placed", and DESIGN gives the hint's fly-in no clip of its own. Assets are read-only, so nothing was added.
- considered: switching to the brass look at the pull and only scaling (simpler, but the material change then comes before the press); a jolt of the rail (the lever already shakes it; the robot's letter needs no rail motion); detecting the lock only on the lever transition (`newlyLocked` covers every way a letter locks and keeps the hint's timing in `hintIn`).
- tests: new `LockedLookTests` (7): `newlyLocked` through node (the lever's o and e, a re-render, a start-over pull, the robot's letter, another block in a locked slot, nothing locked, inputs untouched); the clunk with stand-ins for the DOM and GSAP on easy "horse" checked as "hosre" then the lever (`drag.js returnAll` gives h, o, e locked; o and e get `landing` and the 90ms press, the given h nothing; at the hit `landing` is off and they rise from 0.92; a re-render starts nothing); reduced motion (two opacity dips, no `landing`); nothing clunks on normal's lever, at a new word's start, on a placement, a correct check or a start-over pull; the motion spec; the look in game.css (brass face, ink letter, four rivets at the corners, 86%, inset shadows only, no amber edge, tokens only, only two `.letter-box.locked` rules, cache-buster >= 23); the wiring (card.js sets the class from `isLocked`, no other module sets it, renderFeedback's call and `hint` filter, `hintIn` adds `landing` before `travel` and clunks after it, the helpers never touch state). Updated: `EasyStartTests.test_the_locked_look` (the cursor rule; the look is B-211's), `HintTests.test_the_landing_look` (no override rule; the placed rule draws a landing block), `HintTests.test_the_wrong_letter_hops_back_then_his_letter_flies_in` (the press at 0.9s, the hit at 0.99s, the rise). On the pre-B-211 frontend 6 of the 7 new tests fail (the one that passes guards that nothing clunks without a new lock), and so do the 3 updated ones. 291 tests in all, passing.

## 2026-10-17 — The robot's nudge: `state.nudges`, counted by a main.js timer from the answer; character.js plays `domeNudge` on each one more (B-212)
- chose: state.js `nudges: 0`, the times he has nudged toward the dome on the current word. character.js: `NUDGE_MS = { first: 4000, every: 8000 }` and a pure `nudgeWait(state)`. It gives null off the play screen, while the dome is pressed (`pressed === 'dome'`: the next word is coming), or unless `domeAction(state.current)` is `'next'` (character.js imports it from controls.js; no cycle, since controls.js imports only level.js). Otherwise it gives `first` while `nudges` is 0 and `every` after that. main.js `armNudge()` is called right after the setState that makes the word correct and the one that reveals it (onCheck). It is a `setTimeout` chain like `armIdle`: the word it belongs to (`wordKey`) lives in its closure, each tick adds one to `nudges` through setState and waits `nudgeWait` again, and it ends when that gives null or the word changes. `nudges: 0` is written with every new word (startRound, onNext's next word), at the round end and in onThemes (so also by the back button). `snapshot` gains `nudges` (0 off a word) and `NONE` `nudges: 0`. `momentsFor` returns `['domeNudge']` when `now.nudges > prev.nudges`, after the speaker-tap check; nothing else changes on an answered word.
- chose (the clock): "about 4 seconds" is counted from the answer, the setState that makes the word correct or revealed, not from the last input; "has not pressed it" means the dome only. So a tap on the speaker to hear the word again does not put the nudge off. The nudges repeat every 8s with no cap (the acceptance gives "at most every 8 seconds" and no limit).
- checked in the browser (real flow, 360x740): nudges at 4.0s, 12.0s and 20.0s after a correct check; the dome pressed at 21s: the next word starts with `nudges` 0, and none comes on it in the next 5s; no idle beat throughout. After a reveal (normal, "wolf" checked wrong three times) the first one comes at 4.0s.
- because: CLAUDE.md "Frontend architecture": state only through setState; main.js owns the robot's timers (the kid-idle timer); renderRobot plays a moment only on a transition it detects; audio.js plays `MOMENTS[moment].sound` on those transitions. So a voice line given to `domeNudge` later plays without new code. The count, not a time, is what the robot reacts to, and it keeps each nudge a transition that a re-render cannot replay.
- considered: (a) a render-side loop in character.js like the idle peek (a `gsap.delayedCall` every 8s after the correct moment): no state, but audio.js could never voice it, and game timing would live in the render; (b) a timestamp `answeredAt` in state with `nudgeWait(state, now)` (a second field for one thing; the robot needs the count anyway); (c) counting from `lastInput` (onInput stops recording it once the idle beat came, so it can be stale, and it would make speaker taps put the nudge off).
- reversible: yes.

- chose: `MOMENTS.domeNudge`: at 0 `{ sink: 0, head: 6, eyes: 'wide', brows: 'up', hands: 'point', look: [26, 22], pop: 1 }`, at 0.3 `fx: 'wag'`, at 2.0 `{ hands: 'rest', eyes: 'idle', brows: 'calm', head: 0, look: [0, 0] }`; `sound: null`. Under reduced motion it is the pointing pose only: no pop, no wag (`reducedStep`, `fxFor`).
- because: CHARACTER.md "Poses" names the hand pose "point (at the dome)", and the prototype's "All slots filled" (`hands('point'); look(26,22)`, voice "Press it, press it!") is him pointing at the dome; its first step is `filledEarly`'s point variant. The pop (CHARACTER "Presence": pop up to react) and the wag (the prototype's extra-excited flourish) make each repeat a visible movement at phone size. He goes back to rest so the next nudge is a new gesture; a held point would make the repeats invisible. The moment is over in 2s, well before the next one (8s), and the moments that answer a word are over before the first (correct 1.7s, 3 in a row 1.85s, third miss 0.9s). Its pose changes are 2s apart (CHARACTER: one per 300ms; the existing test covers every moment). The pointing hand stays clear of the speaker's tap area (hand box down to y 656, tap area from 668; RobotLayoutTests measures every pose).
- sound: none. The existing clips are praise (`correct/`), "wrong" lines (`error/`), `celebration/tada.mp3`, "hmm no" (a refusal) and the `swipe/` effects; none says "go on" or "press it". Another praise clip would repeat the correct moment every 8s. Assets are read-only. The voice-line pool in BACKLOG "Later" covers "Press it, press it!".
- noted: the prototype's `point` pose turns the mitten 32° down. At 360x740 the line from his hand (123, 643) to the dome's glass (282, 679) is 13° down, so the finger aims a little low, toward the console's middle, while his head turns and his eyes look at the dome. It reads as "over there", toward the dome. Kept: CHARACTER calls it the dome point, and `HANDS` is checked against the prototype.
- considered: a new dome-aimed hand pose (not in the prototype; the human tunes poses on `design/reference/robot-puppet.html`); two variants at random (one is enough for a repeat every 8s); only a look at the dome (the acceptance allows it, but eyes in a 39px visor are hard to read at phone size).
- reversible: yes (one table row).

## 2026-10-17 — No kid-idle beat on an answered word; the nudges take its place (B-212)
- chose: `idleWait` returns null while `domeAction(state.current)` is `'next'`, so `armIdle`'s timer ends and `idleBeat` stays as it was. While the word is open, the idle beat comes as before.
- because: the press that checks the word is the last input, so 10s later the idle beat would come on an answered word: he would think, sink and peek between the nudges, a nudge at 12s would pop him back up while `idleBeat` stayed `'sunk'`, and the kid's next input would then play a wake. A kid on an answered word is not stuck on the spelling; not going on is what the nudge is for. CHARACTER "Kid idle 10s" (once per word) now means: once per word while it is open.
- considered: keeping both (the clash above); putting the idle beat off until after a few nudges (two idle reactions for one situation).
- reversible: yes (one line).

## 2026-10-17 — The blink and the nudge after a reveal too, not only after a correct check (B-212)
- chose: `next` (the blink and the nudge) whenever the dome means next: after a correct check and after the third miss reveals the word.
- because: the acceptance says "once a word is correct", and its why is that the kid must see at once that the dome means "go on". DESIGN "Controls and states" gives the dome one meaning after a correct answer or a reveal: next. A revealed word waits for the dome just the same. With the old pulse after a reveal, the dome would look like "check" while meaning next, which is the mix-up this item removes (`dome-next-revealed-nudge.png`).
- considered: correct only (after a reveal the dome would keep the "check" pulse).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-17 — The halo's box ends at the play screen's right and bottom edges, so the page does not scroll (B-212, review fix round 1)
- corrects: the first B-212 entry above says the halo "runs under the frame's bezel on the right, as soft light (the B-209 nudge's halo does the same)". That comparison is wrong. B-209's glow is a `box-shadow`, which paints outside its box and adds no scroll area. B-212's halo was a positioned box of its own (197.5x124.6px centred on the dome's glass), and a positioned box adds scroll area even at opacity 0, all the time. Nothing above it (html, body, `#screen-play`) clips overflow. Measured on the round-0 build, play screen open, `scrollTo(200, 200)`: 360x740 scrollWidth/Height 381/741, scrolled 21/1; 390x844 414/847, 24/3; 412x915 439/920, 27/5, on every word. `#screen-play` has `touch-action: pan-y`, so a vertical swipe on the bench moved the page 1–5px under the fixed frame, and any scroll could move it 21–27px sideways (DESIGN "Screen checklist": nothing scrolling sideways).
- chose: the light keeps its size and centre and its box is cut to the screen. `#screen-play .console::after` gets the light's radii as `--rx` (1.2x the dome's width) and `--ry` (1.075x its height). Its box starts `--rx` left of and `--ry` above the glass (`left: calc(81.5% - var(--rx))`, `top: calc(41% - … - var(--ry))`) and ends at the play screen's own right and bottom edges: `right: calc((var(--console-w) - var(--scene-w)) / 2)`, the 18px the console is inset from the column's sides, and `bottom: -22px`, `#screen-play`'s padding below the console, which is pinned to the bottom. The gradient is `radial-gradient(var(--rx) var(--ry) at var(--rx) var(--ry), …)` with the same stops, replacing `closest-side` on a centred box (no `width`, `height` or `transform` any more). Measured after: the box is x 183–360, y 617–740 at 360x740, and ends exactly at the screen's right and bottom edges at 390x844, 412x915, 360x640 and 1024x740 (column centred, box right = column right). scrollWidth/Height equal the window at all five, and `scrollTo(200, 200)` stays at 0, 0. Pixel diff of the dome's corner (x 150–360, y 560–740) against the round-0 shot of the same frozen frame: the off phase is identical; the on phase differs by at most 4/255 per channel (the gradient's dithering in a box of another size), so nothing visible changed.
- first try, dropped: the box ended at the console's right and bottom edges (x 342, y 718). That cut the light with a hard vertical line in the strip between the console's end and the frame's visible bezel, which starts further right than its 26px border box (diff up to 133/255 there).
- considered: (a) `overflow: clip` on `#screen-play` (Chrome 90 / Safari 16, inside the floor; it would also clip anything else that paints past the column, e.g. a block dragged past the 480px column's edge on desktop, a change beyond this item); `overflow: hidden` (it makes a scroll container and breaks the bench's vertical pan on short windows); (b) the light as `box-shadow` layers on a small box centred on the glass (no scroll area, but a box-shadow is not a radial gradient: the core-to-edge stops that make it brightest just outside the rim would have to be rebuilt in blur and spread, a new look to tune again).
- tests: new `NextDomeTests.test_the_halo_adds_no_scroll_area`. It checks the box's right and bottom offsets against the console's inset and `#screen-play`'s padding-bottom (parsed from game.css), that there is no `width`, `height`, `transform` or `closest-side` in the rule, and, in px at 360, 390, 412, 480 and 1024 wide, that the box starts right of the speaker's tap area and ends at the screen's right edge while the light alone would reach past it. `test_the_next_dome_blinks_on_and_off_in_a_light_past_its_rim` now checks the radii, the box's left and top, and the gradient's `at`. On the round-0 CSS both fail. 303 tests in all, passing. The cache-buster stays `?v=24` (round 0 is not shipped).
- screenshots (`.agent/screenshots/B-212/`, re-taken; every run now also asserts that the page has no scroll area, scrollWidth/Height equal to the window and `scrollTo(200, 200)` staying at 0, 0, besides its earlier asserts): taken with tools/shot.mjs itself: `dome-next-on.png`, `dome-next-off.png`, `dome-robot-nudge.png`, `dome-ready.png`, `dome-next-hard-12.png`, `dome-next-revealed-nudge.png` (its waits shortened so it fits the tool's 15s step: 13.7s), and the new `dome-next-on-412x915.png` (`--viewport 412x915`, the same frozen lit frame on a wider phone), plus the zoom sheet `dome-zoom-x2.png` (from the scratch page, regenerated from the new shots). Taken with the throwaway copy of tools/shot.mjs outside the repo, because tools/shot.mjs cannot emulate prefers-reduced-motion: `dome-next-reduced.png` and `dome-robot-nudge-reduced.png` (the copy adds only `Emulation.setEmulatedMedia`, a 45s step timeout and printing the eval result; viewport, scale and capture are the tool's). `before-dome-next.png` (the base commit's frontend, tools/shot.mjs) is unchanged.
- reversible: yes (CSS only).
