## 2026-10-20 — The slot ladder in layout.js: one row up to 8 letters, two rows at the word's break for 9–10, every row centred in a grid of half-slot columns (B-213)
- chose: `slotLayout(count, railWidth, brk)` returns `{ size, gap, pad, font, hit, cols, rows, split, cells, joints }`. 1–6 letters: one row of 46px slots, 6px apart (unchanged). 7–8 letters (`ONE_ROW_MAX`): one row, the `break` ignored, the slots the largest whole px that fits the rail (`MIN_SLOT` 36 to `SLOT` 46), 4px apart (`SMALL_GAP`): 41px for 7 letters and 36px for 8 on the 332px rail of a 360 wide phone. 9–10 letters: two rows of 46px, 6px apart, `split` = [break, rest]; a `break` that does not split the word (`isBreak`: a whole number from 1 to n − 1) or whose longer row would not fit the rail falls back to the even split ([5, 4], [5, 5]). A rail narrower than its rung (under 360 wide) keeps B-113's rule: as many 46px slots as fit on a row, rows as even as possible. The rail is a CSS grid with two columns per slot of the longest row, each (size − gap) / 2 wide; card.js gives each slot `grid-row` and `grid-column: <col> / span 2` from `cells`, so each row is centred and a row one slot shorter sits half a slot in (motor | bike). `--cols` now counts those half columns. At 360: helicopter 4 | 6, submarine 3 | 6, motorbike 5 | 4, rows 46px at y 355 and 407 as before.
- chose (7–8 letters on a wider column): the gap stays 4px even where 46px slots fit (the 480px column, a 412 phone for 7 letters), reading "gap 4px" in the acceptance literally; the size never goes above 46px.
- because: BACKLOG B-213 (decided by the human 2026-10-16). Per-row centring cannot be had from a grid of whole slot columns when two rows differ by an odd count (5 | 4), and the slots must stay the only children of `#letter-boxes`: card.js, feedback.js (wiggle, fly-in, hop) and celebrate.js find slot i as child i.
- considered: flex-wrap with a line-break element (a non-slot child of the rail shifts those indices); absolutely placed slots from computed x / y like the bench spots (more CSS and a second geometry for the rail); a half-slot `margin-left` on a shorter row's slots (overflows its grid cell; fragile under the box pulse); 6px gaps for 7–8 letters when the slots are not shrunk.
- reversible: yes (layout.js, one CSS rule, card.js).

## 2026-10-20 — `break` travels with the word: the data file → main.js → round.js makeWord → `state.current.break`; the missing-break warning in makeWord (B-213)
- chose: main.js startRound copies `data[word].break` into the round's words; round.js `makeWord` puts `break` into the word it sets up (the entry's `break` when it splits the word, else null; state.js documents `current.break: null`) and, for a word of more than 8 letters with none, logs `console.warn('flip: "<word>" has <n> letters and no break; its two slot rows split at half')`, once each time the word is set up. card.js passes `current.break` to `slotLayout`, and so does drag.js for the drop area.
- because: the acceptance asks for the warning; makeWord runs once per word, while layout.js runs on every render (and in drag.js), so a warning there would repeat or need a cache. The theme data already carries `break` (helicopter 4, motorbike 5, submarine 3, scooter 4, bicycle 2), so no test entry was needed for 9–10 letters; a new test checks that every 9–10 letter content word has a `break` whose rows fit six slots. There is no 8-letter word in the data, so `long-8-letters.png` shows a test entry, `airplane` with plane's picture and audio, injected in the screenshot's `--eval` through round.js makeWord (the data files are untouched).
- considered: carrying the whole data entry in state (more than the rail needs); warning in card.js or layout.js (see above).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-20 — Small slots: the letter at 0.58 of the slot, the locked letter as large as its neighbours, a tap and drop area out to the rail's edges (B-213)
- chose (letters): `font` is 28px in a 46px slot (unchanged) and round(0.58 × size) in a smaller one: 24px at 41, 21px at 36; card.js sets it as `--slot-font`. The locked look (B-211) takes `min(25px, var(--slot-font))`: 25px in a 46px slot as before, 21px on the 31px brass plate of a 36px slot, so the given letter and the robot's letter (B-206) are as large as the letters beside them (`long-8-letters.png`: the given a and his i locked, next to placed letters). The plate, its 86% size and its 6px rivets are unchanged.
- chose (tap): `hit` is { x: gap / 2, y: rail padding } for a slot under 46px ({ 2, 8 }), else { 0, 0 }; card.js sets `--hit-x` / `--hit-y`, and a transparent `::before` on a placed block (now `position: relative`) reaches that far past it: 40x52px on a 36px slot (the rail's full height), 45x57px on a 41px one, meeting its neighbour's in the middle of the 4px gap. A tap or a pick-up there is the block's own. Unchanged for 46px slots (inset 0).
- chose (drop): drag.js `nearestHitBox` asks layout.js `dropsIn(block, slot, hit)` instead of `Draggable.hitTest(el, box, '50%')`: the block covers at least half of the smaller of the two, block or slot, with the slot's area (`slotArea`: the slot grown by `hit`). With hit 0 that is exactly GSAP's rule, so 46px slots behave as before; on a small slot it only adds (a test checks both). Nearest centre still wins.
- because: the acceptance (tap and drop area the full rail height, so a kid's finger still finds a 36–40px slot). Testing GSAP's hitTest against the grown rectangle was the first build; its "half of either area" against the larger area would have made some drops stricter (a 26x26px corner overlap lands on a 36px slot today and would have needed 1040px²), so the overlap is measured in layout.js, pure and node-tested.
- checked in the browser (360x740, airplane on normal, pointer events in `--eval`): taps on bench blocks fill the first empty slots; `elementFromPoint` 7px above and below slot 2 and 1.5px into the gap on its left gives its letter; a pointer down and up 7px above the slot (in the rail's padding) sends the letter back; a block dragged from the bench and let go with its centre 30px below slot 4's centre lands in slot 4 (under the old rule its overlap was 576px² of the needed 648).
- noted: DESIGN's checklist asks tap targets of 48px or more ("slots may be 46px"); a 36–41px slot's tap area is 40–45px wide and 52–57px tall, which is what B-213 sets (the full rail height; across, eight slots on a 316px row leave no more). Suggested in the last entry.
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-20 — The brass joint: two `.rail-joint` spans in the slots at the break, drawn in one game.css rule set, lit once by feedback.js (B-213)
- chose (look, provisional): a 9x22px brass knuckle, lit from the upper left (`--brass-hi` → `--brass` → `--brass-dark`), rimmed in `--brass-dark`, a `--cream` pin with a dark ring in its middle, rounded on its outer side and set 1px off its slot with a contact shadow. `.rail-joint.end` stands out right of the first row's last slot, `.rail-joint.start` left of the second row's first slot, vertically centred on the slot; two halves of one hinge. Everything about it is the four rules `.rail-joint`, `.rail-joint.end`, `.rail-joint.start`, `.rail-joint::after`, so the human can restyle or replace it there. At 360 the start half beside a six-slot row (heli | copter, sub | marine) keeps 3px of brass to the rail's edge (a test holds every split whose rows fit, at 360 and 480, to 2px or more and clear of the other slots).

The joint is not a child of `#letter-boxes` (see the ladder entry).

## 2026-10-20 — The tune-in's drawing: the prototype's tuneIn ported number for number, rolling the picture as the screen shows it (B-214)
- chose: `TUNE` holds every number of tv-motion.html `tuneIn` (1000ms; picture in 230→720ms eased, shining 18% over until 900; snow −78% eased 230→700, the rest linearly by 930; roll and tear settle 230→760; roll 1.6 screens; tear 14px, sine 0.11 a row and 0.02 a ms; jolts 4% of rows × 30px; black gap 8% while settle > 0.02; sync bar 12% tall, dimmed to 0.35, light edge ±1.5 rows (half + 120), 0.045 of the height a frame × (0.4 + settle), looping at 1.25 heights; grain keeps 0.42 of its left neighbour, power 1.35, row gain 0.7 + up to 0.55, tint r 0.88 g 0.97 b 1; snow under the picture × 0.7; one canvas row per 1.5 CSS px, at least 120 × 60; canvas fade-out 120ms; hiss 0.95 of the tune-in). `drawTune` is its per-pixel loop, pure, on byte arrays; at 1000ms it is the picture exactly. Where the game is not the prototype page: (a) the picture rolled is the one the screen shows: once `#play-image` has loaded, tv.js draws it into a W × H canvas the way game.css frames it (the tube's radial gradient, `--teal` to `--teal` × 0.85 = `--teal-lo`, ellipse at 50% 45%, farthest corner; then the picture at its object-fit: cover for the new `.webp` pictures, contain in its rounded square for the old ones, `duck.svg` today), so the last frame is the screen's picture and the canvas fades off it without a jump; (b) the sync bar's step is scaled by the frame's length (dt / 16.7ms), so it rolls as fast at 120 Hz as at 60; (c) the clock is the sum of the frames' lengths (so it can hold, next entry), not `now − t0`; (d) the first frame is drawn at once, in the subscriber, before the browser paints the new picture under the canvas (the prototype hid its picture layer instead); (e) GSAP fades the canvas off and clears its inline opacity. The prototype's WAAPI fade with `fill: "forwards"` stays in effect and holds its canvas at opacity 0 for every later tune-in, so on that page only the tune-in after a load shows its snow; noted, not copied. (f) The snow's grain keeps the prototype's cool grey (up to rgb 224, 247, 255), drawn in code on the canvas: a picture texture like the word picture, not a scene or UI colour. This entry is the palette's "DECISIONS entry" for it.
- considered: tinting the grain to the palette (cream / ink gives a sepia snow, not the approved look); rolling the picture without the tube (an old picture would roll on black and the tube pop in at the lock); reading the tube colours from the computed gradient (browsers serialise `color-mix()` differently; `--teal` is a plain hex in :root).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-20 — A picture still loading holds the snow; the next word's picture is fetched ahead (B-214)
- chose: every frame tv.js checks whether `#play-image` has loaded; until it has, the tune-in's clock stops at 230ms (where the picture starts to come in) while the snow and the bar go on, for at most `TUNE.hold` 2000ms; then the TV tunes in to no picture (the prototype's no-picture path: the snow fades to dark and the canvas fades off whatever the picture shows by then). A picture that failed to load, or a canvas that will not give its pixels back, goes on at once without it. On each new word tv.js starts loading the round's next picture (`new Image().src`), so from the second word on the hold seldom comes. Under reduced motion the fade waits the same way (load or error event, 2s at most).
- because: the prototype decoded its two pictures before its first tune-in; in the game the picture starts loading in the same render as the tune-in and over a phone network is often not there by 230ms. Without the hold the first word would roll no picture and the picture would pop in after it. Holding longer than 2s would look broken.
- considered: no hold (above); waiting for the picture before showing the play screen (changes the round's flow; outside this item); preloading all five pictures at the round start (the next one is enough).
- reversible: yes (`TUNE.hold`).

- chose: `hiss(ms)` in audio.js is the prototype's `hiss()` with its numbers in `HISS` / `hissEnvelope`: noise 0.1s longer than the hiss, the click [1, −1, 0.8] in its first samples, high-pass 1400 Hz, low-pass 7000 Hz, gain 0.0001 → 0.09 in 20ms, held to 45%, back to 0.0001 at the end, the source stopped 50ms later; 950ms long (0.95 of the tune-in). It plays on audio.js's one AudioContext (made on the first gesture), straight out, not through the robot's analyser (it is not his voice), as `CHANNEL.hiss = 'fx'` (it may overlap anything; it neither cuts nor is cut by his voice or the word), and it joins `live`, so the mute stops it. Nothing while muted or before the first gesture; a round always starts with a tap, so the first word's hiss plays. tv.js calls it as a tune-in starts. Under reduced motion there is no tune-in and no hiss (the prototype returns before `hiss()` there). The nodes are connected one by one, not chained.

## 2026-10-22 — The lever's two facts: `rightAtCheck` per slot from a pure `checkedWrong`, `justPulled` cleared by every block move and by a wrong check (B-215b)
- chose: `current.rightAtCheck` is an array with one entry per slot, the tile id of the block the word's last wrong check found right there (its letter is the word's letter at that slot), else null; the whole field is null before any wrong check and after a start-over pull. It is set by a new pure drag.js `checkedWrong(current)` (status `'wrong'`, `rightAtCheck`, `justPulled: false`), which main.js `onCheck` applies to every wrong check that leaves lives, before the second miss's `giveHint` (so the robot's slot, wrong or empty at the check, is null; his letter stays anyway). The given letter and letters a pull locked are in it when right, as any right slot is. `current.justPulled` is set true by `returnAll` (every pull, also one that sends nothing back) and false by `placeIn`, `takeOut` and `giveHint` (the robot's letter counts as landing when its state changes, not when the fly-in ends) and by `checkedWrong`; `revealed` leaves both alone (the lever is off after a reveal). `makeWord` starts a word with `rightAtCheck: null, justPulled: false`; state.js documents both on `current`.
- because: the acceptance ("the tile ids the word's last wrong check found right, per slot"; "false after any block move (drag, tap or a robot letter landing)"). A per-slot array answers "still in its own slot" with one comparison. A pure helper keeps the rule testable through `node` and the state change in main.js's one `setState`. Clearing `justPulled` on a wrong check keeps "right after a pull" literal: a new check between two pulls makes the second one judged by that check, not a start-over (it cannot happen today, since a wrong check needs a move after a pull, but the field should not depend on that).
- considered: a flat list of tile ids (loses which slot was checked: a right block moved into another slot of the same letter would count as checked there); computing the list inline in main.js (untestable without the DOM); leaving `justPulled` alone on a check.
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-22 — The lever rule on every stage, judged by block, not by letter (B-215b)
- chose: drag.js `leverResult(current)` and `returnAll(current)`, no level or stage argument, PROGRESSION.md "Lever rule": with `justPulled`, start over (every placed letter back, ones a pull locked too; `returnAll` clears `rightAtCheck`); otherwise with `rightAtCheck`, a placed block stays and locks only if it is the block `rightAtCheck` names for its slot, and every other one goes back; with neither, every placed letter goes back. Kept out of both lists: the given letter (its tray tile is `given`), the robot's letter (`hint`) and a boost's letter (`boost`, B-219; added now since the acceptance names it, so B-219 only has to set the flag). Consequences, all in the tests: a right block taken out and put back in its own slot stays; a block of the same letter put in that slot after the check goes back (the sheep's other e); a right letter placed after the check goes back (never a free check); after a pull and a move, the next pull keeps the letters the first pull locked (they are still in their own slot) and sends the new ones back, where B-205 sent everything back; after a start-over every pull sends everything back until the next wrong check. `returnAll` still reopens a wrong check (`afterMove`) as before. feedback.js needed no change: its hop and clunk key on the slots, so they follow the new rule.
- because: the acceptance and PROGRESSION.md "Lever rule" ("the lever never judges a letter the check didn't"); judging by tile id is what "the tile ids ... per slot" and "the blocks in `rightAtCheck` that are still in their own slot" say.
- considered: judging by letter (a same-letter block swapped in after the check would be kept, a letter the check never saw there); keeping B-205's "any pull after a move sends everything back" (the acceptance makes only a pull right after a pull start over).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-22 — progress.js: solved words and cleared stages per theme; only the round's stage clears (B-216)
- chose: `static/js/progress.js` (no DOM, no module state; every read and write in try/catch, as missed.js): `KEY = 'flip.progress.v1'`, `load()` (sanitised: a theme whose value is not an object is dropped; `solved` keeps unique non-empty strings; `cleared` keeps STAGES values only, in STAGES order), `forTheme(theme)` (`{ solved: [], cleared: [] }` for a theme never finished), `record(theme, data, stage, results)` (writes only when the theme's progress changes) and the pure `currentStage(data, progress)` and `afterRound(progress, data, stage, results)`. `currentStage`: the first stage of `stagesOf(data)` not in `cleared`; all cleared, the last; data with no word in any stage, stage 1 (as B-215's `?? STAGES[0]`). `afterRound` returns `{ progress, justCleared }` (new arrays, inputs untouched): every result with `correct === true` joins `solved` (a word solved with the robot's letter is correct; a revealed one is not); `stage` joins `cleared` once every word of that stage in `data` is solved; a stage with no word in `data`, or a value that is not a stage, never clears. Only the round's own stage can clear: words of other stages solved in the round (review or fill) count as solved and clear their stage at the end of its own round. Consequence with today's partial data: a stage whose words were all solved as fill clears at the end of its first round whatever its results (animals: frog, stage 2's only word, is in every stage-1 round; so after a clean stage 1, stage 2 clears in one round). `round-end-not-cleared.png` therefore has frog revealed in both rounds.
- because: the acceptance ("adds `stage` to `cleared` once all of its words in the data are solved, and says whether it was just cleared"; a cleared stage stays cleared) and PROGRESSION.md "Stages" (stages open in order; a stage with no words is skipped). Clearing a stage that has no word would mark something the kid never played.
- considered: clearing every stage whose words are all solved after any round (could clear stage 3 before stage 1, against "open in order"); a boolean-only return (the round-end also needs the cleared list); filtering `solved` to the data's words (a word dropped from the data later is harmless in the list).
- reversible: yes.

The round-leaving setState that `onThemes` had is now `leaveRound(patch)`: `onThemes` is `leaveRound({ screen: 'theme', theme: null })` (the round-end's ▦ and the stage screen's new back arrow, `#stages-back-btn`), and the play screen's back arrow (`onPlayBack`, B-201) takes its stickers out as before and then `leaveRound({ screen: 'stages' })`, keeping the theme for its map. Unchanged: play again (▶) plays the theme's current stage (B-216), also after a replay of a cleared stage; the round-end's ▦ goes to the theme screen; the sticker book stays on the theme screen.

## 2026-10-23 — The theme screen and the stage map render from state: `state.themes[].stages` at load, `state.progress` a copy of progress.js `load()` (B-217)
- chose: main.js `loadThemes`, which already fetches every theme's data file for its word list, adds `stages: stagesOf(data)` to each theme in `state.themes`, so the theme cards and the stage map know a theme's stages without another fetch. The cleared stages come from a new `state.progress` (`{}` in state.js): progress.js `load()` (every theme's `{ solved, cleared }`, sanitised), set by main.js at start-up and again in `onNext`'s round-end branch, in the same setState as the round end, right after progress.js `record`. progress.js stays the only reader and writer of the stored key; `startRound` still reads `forTheme(name)` from it, as B-216 built. The theme cards' bulb rows and stagemap.js read only state, so a card shows a round's clear as soon as the kid is back on the theme screen.

Refreshing the copy with `load()` rather than merging `record`'s return keeps it equal to what `startRound` and play again read: with storage blocked both show no progress, as B-216 does today; a merge would show a cleared stage the next round's `record` (which starts from the store) would forget.

- chose: a pure `stations(stages, progress)` in progress.js: one `{ stage, cleared, start, open }` per stage of `stages` (in STAGES order, non-stages dropped), `cleared` from the progress's sanitised `cleared`, `start` the first stage not cleared or, with all cleared, the last one, `open` = cleared or the start. A stage the data adds before a cleared one (transportation's empty stage 2, if it gets words) becomes the start while the cleared stage after it stays open. `afterRound` is unchanged: only the round's own stage can join `cleared`, and a replayed stage already has, so a replay records its solved words (help counts, as always) and clears nothing new, also when its review or fill words complete a later stage (that stage clears at the end of its own round, B-216's documented consequence); a test runs this through node.

## 2026-10-23 — gears.js: the count in `flip.gears.v1`, `add` only, and the pure rules `isClean`, `wordGears`, `roundGears` (B-218)
- chose: `static/js/gears.js` (no DOM, no module state; every read and write in try/catch, as progress.js): `KEY = 'flip.gears.v1'`, the count stored as JSON (`"12"`); `load()` returns it when it is a safe whole number, 0 or more, else 0 (missing, bad JSON, `"5"` as a string, `-3`, `2.5`, `null`, an array or object, `1e400`); `add(n)` adds a whole number above 0 and returns the stored count after it (`load()`), and anything else (0, negative, fractional, a string, NaN, Infinity, above the safe range) adds and writes nothing. No function takes gears away (never lost; B-219's boosts will spend). `GEARS = { correct: 1, clean: 1, round: 2 }`. Pure: `isClean(correct, wrongChecks, boosted = false)` (correct, no wrong check before it, no boost), `wordGears(result)` (1 for `correct: true`, 1 more for `clean: true`, else 0), `roundGears(results)` (the words' gears plus `GEARS.round`, the round end's "+N"). With storage blocked `add` returns 0, so the counters show 0, as B-217's progress shows nothing then (the state copy always equals the store).
- because: the acceptance (the only reader and writer of the key, a whole number, 0 or more, in try/catch; pure helpers for a word result's gears and a finished round's) and PROGRESSION.md "Gears" (1 per correct word, +1 clean, +2 per finished round; never lost; mistakes never cost gears). `wrongChecks` is the lives bulbs lost (`LIVES - lives`): every wrong check costs one and a correct check ends the word, so a correct check with every bulb lit is the word's first check; no new field on `current` is needed. B-219 passes `boosted`.
- considered: returning `load() + n` from `add` even when the write failed (the counter would go up and fall back on the next add with storage blocked); a `checks` counter on `current` (the bulbs already say it); `isClean` taking `lives` (ties gears.js to LIVES); a `spend`/`remove` now (not asked; B-219).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-23 — The cogs' flight: celebrate.js, one cog per gear, the count held and flipped up as each lands, all within 600ms (B-218)
- chose: in celebrate.js (CLAUDE.md: the only place the correct-check celebrations run), on the transition to `'correct'`, `flyGears(prev.gears, state.gears, wordGears(result))`: one 22px cog per gear the word earns (2 for a clean word, 1 otherwise) in a fixed, click-through `.gear-fly` layer like the sparks'. Each pops out of the TV picture's centre at half size, rises 22px and spreads (the two 18px apart) in 100ms (`power1.out`), then drops into the console counter's cog in 260ms (`power2.in`), turning and shrinking to 0.8; the second sets off 100ms after the first. Until a cog lands the counter holds the count from before the word (celebrate's render cache keeps the last `state.gears` it saw), written with cogs.js `showCount`, as B-216's `popBulb` holds a bulb dark until the stars finish; as each lands the count goes one step up (`gearTicks(from, to, n)`, even steps ending at state's count), the flaps that changed (`changedFlaps`, compared from the right) flip up (scaleY 0 → 1, 120ms) and the counter's cog turns to its next tooth (45°, so clearing the turn shows no jump); the cog and the layer are removed when they land and every tween clears what it set (transform and transform-origin). A clean word: landings at 360ms and 460ms, the last flip done at 580ms (`gearsDone(2)`; one cog: 480ms), inside the acceptance's 600ms; measured in the browser: "0" → "1" at ~362ms, "2" at ~442ms, no layer and no inline style left after. Leaving the correct word (the next word, the round end, the back arrow) mid-flight drops the cogs, stops the flips and shows `state.gears` (`settleGears`). Reduced motion: no cogs and no flip; cogs.js has already shown the new count (checked with an emulated `prefers-reduced-motion`). The numbers are motion.js `gearFly` (`duration 0.36, ease power2.in, stagger 0.1, rise 0.1, height 22, size 22, spread 18`) and `gearFlip` (`duration 0.12, ease power2.out`). No sound and no robot moment (a clink is in "Later / not now").
- because: the acceptance (on a correct word small cogs fly from the TV to the counter and the count flips up, 600ms at most; reduced motion: the count just changes). One cog per gear makes the clean bonus visible; flipping per landing reads as the cogs adding up.
- considered: a new module for the flight (CLAUDE.md gives correct-check celebrations to celebrate.js); flipping the count at once as the cogs set off (the acceptance's order is fly, then flip); a curved path through GSAP's MotionPathPlugin (a new CDN dependency); 18px cogs (lost among the 6px sparks in the first look).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-23 — boost.js: the rules, and the boost button on only after every slot has held a block once (B-219)
- chose: a new `static/js/boost.js` (no DOM, no module state; imports `isLocked`, `afterMove`, `markFilled` from drag.js): `BOOSTS` (`['remember', 'first', 'cleanup']`, the tray's order), `COSTS` (`{ remember: 3, first: 2, cleanup: 2 }`), `PER_WORD` (1); `offered(current)` (remember when a slot isn't locked, first when slot 0 isn't locked, cleanup while a block marked `extra` and not `gone` is on the bench or in a slot) and `pickable(current)` (the unlocked slots), both from the locks and the extra marks only, never from a placed letter; `tried(current)`, `boostOn(current)` (a word, status `playing` or `wrong`, fewer than `PER_WORD` boosts (`current.boosts`), tried, and something offered), `canAfford(gears, kind)`, `isBoosted(current)`. "Every slot of the current word has been filled at least once" is read per slot: a new `current.filledOnce` (one flag per slot; round.js `makeWord` starts it from `placed`, so stage 1's given slot is already true, and drag.js `placeIn` marks the slot through a pure `markFilled`). The button is also off when no boost applies (every slot locked and no extra block), as a tap would open an empty tray.

- considered: "the whole word filled at once" as the first try (stricter than the acceptance's wording); deriving it from existing state (not possible once the lever empties slots); `boosted` as a boolean on `current` (a count keeps `PER_WORD` tunable, PROGRESSION.md "start at 1").
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-23 — Which block a boost places, and what else a boost changes; Clean-up keeps the blocks' spots (B-219)
- chose: `letterFor(current, index)`: the block already in the slot when it has the slot's letter (it stays and locks: "also when the right letter was already there", and it still costs); else a block with that letter on the bench (the first in tray order); else the block holding it in the first slot, left to right, that is not locked (that slot empties); the slot's block, if any, goes back to its bench spot. `giveLetter(current, index)` puts it there as `{ letter, tileId, locked: true, boost: true }`, one more `boosts`, `justPulled` false (a block moved), the status through drag.js `afterMove` only when the arrangement changed (a letter that only locks leaves a wrong check's `wrong` as it was, so the same arrangement can't be checked twice). First letter is `giveLetter(current, 0)`. `cleanUp(current)`: every extra block still in play leaves its slot and is marked `gone` (and not `used`) in the tray, one more `boosts`, the status reopened only if a slot changed. A gone block stays in `current.tray`, so card.js still lays out a bench spot for it and the other blocks keep theirs; drag.js `placeIn` refuses it; the lever, the reveal and the hint already skip extras.
- because: the acceptance ("the robot puts the right letter there ... also when the right letter was already there"; "the other blocks keep their spots") and the never-a-free-check rule: the place a block comes from is chosen by position, never by whether a letter is right there, so with a repeated letter ("book") the boost never tells which of two o's was right (it may take a right o out of its slot; the kid puts it back). The free hint (`hint`) is kept apart from `boost`, so drag.js `hintFor` skips the locked boost slot and the second miss still gives its letter.
- considered: drag.js `hintFor`'s choice (never empties a right slot, but picks the source by rightness: a free check with repeated letters); hopping the already-right block out and back in (the same block, no point); removing gone blocks from the tray (the bench would re-lay out and every block would move).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-23 — Paying: gears.js `spend` when the boost happens; can't afford: `lockedTaps` + 1; results record `boosted` (B-219)
- chose: gears.js `spend(n)` (a whole number above 0): takes `n` and returns the count after it, or null with nothing taken or written when the store has fewer, for any other `n`, or when the write did not stick (storage blocked). main.js: `onBoost` (the button; ignored while a control is held, `state.pressed`, or the tray is open) sets `state.boostMenu: 'tray'`; `onBoostPick(kind)` checks the boost is still on and offered, then `canAfford(state.gears, kind)`: not enough is `{ boostMenu: null, lockedTaps: +1 }` (nothing spent: the robot's `speakerLocked` head-shake with "hmm no", and the counter's flicker); "Remember this one" goes to `boostMenu: 'pick'`; First letter and Clean-up are paid at once. `onBoostSlot(index)` (an unlocked slot) pays "Remember this one". `pay(kind, next)` calls `spend` and sets `{ boostMenu: null, gears, current: next }` in one setState (or, should `spend` say no, the can't-afford setState). `onBoostClose` (the scrim) sets `boostMenu: null`, free. `boostMenu` is also reset by `startRound`, both branches of `onNext` and `leaveRound`. `onCheck` writes `boosted: isBoosted(current)` on every checked word and passes it to `isClean`, so a boosted word is solved (B-216), earns its correct gear and no clean gear and no sticker (B-218). Checked in the browser: a stage-1 round with "Remember this one" on the first word (9 gears): 9 − 3 + 1 + 4 × 2 + 2 = 17, round end "+11" and four stickers, frog solved with no sticker (`round-end-boosted.png`).
- because: the acceptance ("the gears are taken when the boost happens"; "not enough gears: nothing is spent and the tray closes; lockedTaps goes up by one"; "boosted: true ... no clean bonus and no sticker") and gears.js staying the only reader and writer of the count. The tray offers the boosts that apply whether or not the kid can afford them: the acceptance's can't-afford reaction comes from tapping one.

- chose: a new `static/js/boosttray.js` (the tray's one component, the M4 header's "one small, easily replaced" piece; subscribed after card.js and before feedback.js, which pops it open): it sets `#console-gears`'s `disabled` from `boostOn` and, while `state.boostMenu` is `'tray'` or `'pick'` (and the button is on), shows `#boost-layer`, a new body-level element in the template after the sticker book (`#boost-scrim`, `#boost-tray`, `#boost-picks`; new ids, not on CLAUDE.md's protected list). The tray is placed from the console's and the button's rects: just above the console's top edge (2px under its notch), centred over the boost button but never over the console's left third (+2.5%, the robot) nor within 6px of its right end, its notch clamped 16px inside it and pointing at the button. ## 2026-10-24 — round.js `swapForStruggle`: the run is the last three results, "not clean" is `clean` not true (B-220)
- chose: a pure `swapForStruggle(words, index, results, data, stage, solved = [], rand = Math.random)` in round.js, with `STRUGGLE_RUN = 3` and `STRUGGLE_SWAP = 2` exported. `words` is `state.words` (entries `{ word, image, audio, break }`), `index` the word just done (`round.index`), `results` the round's results with that word's included, `data` the theme's data file, `stage` the round's stage, `solved` the theme's solved words. The run is the results at `index - 2 … index`: each must exist and have `clean` not `true` (gears.js `isClean`: a wrong check, a boost, or a reveal; a word left without a result, which onNext fills as `{ correct: false, clean: false }`, counts too). It returns a new array with the swapped entries (the other entries are the same objects), or the same array when nothing changes. No marker on a swapped-in entry and no new state field.
- because: results already record `clean` (B-218) and `boosted` (B-219), so no new fact is needed; "three words in a row" read literally is the three words played last. Returning the same array keeps main.js's setState a no-op for `words` when nothing happens, and a test can check "nothing happens" by identity.
- considered: counting only reveals or only wrong checks as "not clean" (PROGRESSION.md names all three); a `swapped` flag on entries or `round.swapped` in state (not needed, see the next entry); taking the word names and returning names (main.js would then rebuild the entries).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-24 — Which words swap in, how many, and how often (B-220)
- chose: the candidates are the theme's words that are in `solved`, whose data `stage` is a stage before the round's (STAGES order, so for `long` stages 1–5), and that are not in the round's words yet (shown or still to come). They go in the nearest earlier stage first, each stage's words at random (pickWords' review order). The places are the next `STRUGGLE_SWAP` words not yet shown (`index + 1`, `index + 2`, as many as the round has left). A word in those places that already is a solved word of an earlier stage (a review word, or one swapped in before) stays where it is. With fewer candidates than places, the first places are swapped and the others keep their words; with none, nothing changes. There is no once-per-round counter: a word swapped in is never swapped out again (it is already "easy"), and the candidates do not change during the round (progress is recorded only at its end), so in a 5-word round the help changes at most the fourth and fifth words, once. In a longer round a later run of three would swap again.
- because: PROGRESSION.md "Guardrails" says "solved words from the theme's earlier stages" with no order; the nearest earlier stage is the order the round already uses for review words, and keeps the swapped-in words close to the stage being learned. Keeping a word that already qualifies avoids replacing an easy word with another easy word. "None when there is nothing solved to swap in" suggests doing what can be done when there is a little.
- considered: the earliest stage first (easier still, but stage-1 words on a stage-5 round are far from the lesson); all earlier stages at random; skipping the swap when fewer than two candidates exist; an explicit once-per-round limit or a reset of the run after a swap (both the same as the chosen rule for 5-word rounds); leaving out solved words that are also on the theme's missed list (would need the missed list as another argument; left for play-testing).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-24 — main.js applies it in onNext; swapped-in words play by the round's stage rules; nothing records the words swapped out (B-220)
- chose: in onNext's next-word branch, `const words = swapForStruggle(state.words, round.index, results, state.data, round.stage, progressFor(state.theme).solved);`, then one setState with `words`, the round's new index and `current: makeWord(words[nextIndex], state.round.stage, newTileId)`. The round-end branch, startRound and every other handler are unchanged. `solved` comes from progress.js `forTheme`, as startRound reads it. A swapped-in word starts by `stageRules(round.stage)` like every word of the round (a stage-2 word on a stage-3 round gets stage 3's extra block). A swapped-out word has no result, so the round end records nothing about it: progress.js does not count it solved (its stage cannot clear this round), missed.js keeps a missed word where it was, and pickRound brings it back (the stage's unsolved words, and up to two missed words, come first). The round end lists the words played, so the swapped-in ones show there like any other. No moment, sound, motion or mark goes with the swap (the kid isn't told). Known consequence: tv.js fetches the next word's picture ahead when a word starts, so a word swapped in as the next word starts was not fetched ahead; its tune-in holds the snow while the picture loads (2s at most, B-214). tv.js is not changed; its comment says so.
- because: BACKLOG B-220 ("main.js applies it before the next word"; "Swapped-out words stay unsolved and come back in later rounds"); PROGRESSION.md "Stages": a round's stage rules apply to every word in it, review and fill words included. One setState means the robot, the TV and the card see the new word once. `forTheme` reads the same store `state.progress` copies; startRound uses it too.
- considered: `state.progress[theme].solved` (the same data; kept to the accessor startRound uses); putting the swapped-out words on the missed list so the next round brings them back for sure (they were not missed, and the missed list only holds words played and not checked correct); the swapped-in word's own stage rules (PROGRESSION.md says the round's rules apply to every word); prefetching the swapped-in picture (a tv.js change outside the item).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-24 — audio.js reads static/voice/manifest.json once at init; pure helpers; a missing or broken manifest lists nothing (B-223)
- chose: `init` fetches `MANIFEST_URL` (`/static/voice/manifest.json`, a plain GET) once, beside the prototype pools' downloads. `listedClips(manifest)` is the keys of its `clips` map; anything else (a 404, JSON that fails to parse, a response without `json()`, a fetch that throws, `clips` missing, an array or not an object) lists none, through `catch` on every step and no `console.error`. New pure exports in audio.js: `VOICE_BASE`, `MANIFEST_URL`, `listedClips`, `voiceUrl(file)` (each path part URL-encoded), `poolOf(file)` (`robot/<id>-NN.mp3` → the id without `-NN`; any other file null, also a `robot/` file without `-NN`), `poolClips(files, pool)`, `gagClips(files, theme, word)` (`gags/<theme>/<word>.mp3` and `gags/<theme>/<word>-2.mp3`, each only when listed; none without a theme or a word), `linePools()` (the pools the moment table's `lines` name, below) and `sayPlan` (below). The list (`listed`) is an audio engine handle kept beside `bytes` and `buffers`, not state: it is content metadata like `FILES`, read once, never changed, and nothing decides game logic from it. The theme folder is `state.theme` (the data's theme names are the voice folders' names: animals, transportation, food, home).
- because: BACKLOG B-223 ("fetches … once at init (a missing or broken manifest counts as an empty one, no console error)"; "a shared line's file is `robot/<line id>.mp3` and its pool is the id without its `-NN`"). A 404 still shows the browser's own network line in DevTools, which no script can prevent; the manifest is in the repo, so the game never asks for a missing one.
- considered: keeping the list in state (nothing but audio.js reads it, and it never changes); fetching it on the first gesture (a fetch needs no gesture, and later is only slower).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-24 — The say: the word, then the line on its `ended`, both Web Audio sources through his analyser; a clip not ready is skipped (B-223)
- chose: on `correct` or `streak` audio.js `say` plays the word's decoded buffer (kind `word`) and, from that source's `onended` when it played to its end, the line (kind `line`, or the prototype pool's kind when no line exists). `word` and `line` are on the voice channel (`CHANNEL`), so they pass the AnalyserNode and he talks with them (scope mouth, eyes, cap, head bob, B-210); a stopped source is taken out of `live` before it is stopped, so its `onended` starts nothing. A clip not decoded at the check is skipped, never waited for, and nothing starts when it decodes later: with the word not ready (still decoding, failed to download, or no `audio`) the line comes at once; with a gag not ready, the choice goes on to the next group in `lines`. The speaker's `<audio>` element cannot reach the analyser without a MediaElementSource, which B-210 kept out (it must play whatever the context's state); a decoded buffer also starts at once. A word not ready happens only when its clip failed or the check came within a few hundred ms of the word appearing; the line alone is what a kid heard before B-223 (the correct clip at once). CHARACTER.md "Rules" (the word, then a quirk) holds whenever the word can be said.
- considered: playing the word through `speakWord` and then the line (he would not show the word); waiting for a word still decoding (the acceptance forbids it); playing nothing when the word is not ready (silence on a correct answer).

## 2026-10-24 — The picture's life lives in a new picture.js, after renderTv; it reads the motion from `state.data`; the layers come on at the lock (B-221)
- chose: `static/js/picture.js`, a state subscriber right after tv.js (`subscribe(renderPicture)` after `subscribe((s) => renderTv(s, onTuned))` in main.js). It keys on the word as tv.js does (`roundsStarted:index:word` on the play screen) and reads the word's `motion` from `state.data[word]` (the round's data file, already in state), through the pure `lifeOf(entry)`. The layers are new elements in the template, inside `.card` between `#play-image` and the tune-in canvas: `.tv-life` (aria-hidden, `pointer-events: none`, `visibility: hidden` at rest) holding `img.tv-plate`, `.tv-motes` and `.tv-actor` > `img.tv-subject` (the prototype's stage: plate, motes, actor). On a new word picture.js cancels the old life, takes `life-on` off the card and sets the plate's and the subject's `src` (and the subject's pivot); once `state.tuned` is true (tv.js's lock, B-214) and both pictures have decoded (`HTMLImageElement.decode()`), it puts `life-on` on `.card`: game.css then shows `.tv-life` and hides `#play-image` under it. So the tune-in and the reduced-motion fade still work on `image` exactly as before, the robot's "Picture shown" still comes from the same `tuned` change (picture.js never sets state), and a picture still loading at the lock just keeps the still picture until it has decoded (the swap is invisible: the layers start at rest and line up with it; measured on screen at 3x, best match at offset 0, 0). A layer that fails to load keeps the still picture. The next word's two layers are fetched ahead (`new Image().src`, as tv.js does for its picture).
- because: the acceptance (a subscriber after `renderTv`; the layers show once the tune-in locks; the tune-in still draws from `image`; no state outside state.js). `state.data` already holds every entry of the round (the struggle help's swapped-in words too), so no field had to be added to the round's words or to `current`.
- considered: carrying `motion` on `state.words` and `current` (main.js startRound, round.js makeWord and swapForStruggle, and their tests, for a field only the picture reads); showing the layers from the word's start under the canvas (the reduced-motion fade animates `#play-image`, and a layer still loading would show a hole or a doubled subject); building the layers in picture.js (tv.js's canvas and bar are in the template; the template keeps the TV's stacking order in one place).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-24 — The idle table: the prototype's sway and rumble, six new moves that start and end at rest; the pivots (B-221)
- chose: one table, `IDLE`, Element.animate keyframes and timing per move, looped forever (`idleOf`). `sway` and `rumble` are tv-motion.html's `IDLE` as they are (sway rotate ±1.8° 3.2s alternate ease-in-out; rumble's four keyframes, 950ms ease-in-out); sway alone also gets `iterationStart: 0.5`, so it starts halfway through its first swing, at 0°, instead of snapping to −1.8° as the layers come in (its keyframes, duration, direction and easing are unchanged). The new ones, each starting and ending at rest, every keyframe with the same transform functions, a keyframe's easing running to the next: `bob` translateY 0 → −1.2% → 0, 1.8s, ease-in-out; `hop` up 1.8% (ease-out, stretched 0.99/1.01), down (ease-in), a squash on landing (scale 1.03/0.97), back to rest by 36% of 1.6s, then a pause; `wobble` a rock that dies down, rotate 0 → −2.5° → 2.2° → −1.3° → 0.6° → 0 by 76% of 2.4s, then a rest; `float` translateY 0 → −2% with rotate 0 → −1.5° and back, 3.4s, ease-in-out; `breathe` scale 1 → 1.02 → 1, 3.2s, ease-in-out; `spin` rotate 0 → 10° → −10° → 0, 3.5s (ease-out, ease-in-out, ease-in, so it never stops at the middle). A % in translate is of the TV picture's box (151 x 110 px at 360). `PIVOTS`: `top` 50% 12%, `bottom` 50% 88%, `center` 50% 50% (the backlog's numbers; the prototype's bat used 51% 14%, its taxi 50% 88%), as `transform-origin` of `.tv-actor`, a box the size of the TV picture. A move the table does not have leaves the subject still (the glow and the specks still run); a pivot it does not have turns from the centre. Every `idle` and `pivot` in today's data is in the table (a test reads the data).
- because: the acceptance ("`sway` and `rumble` are the prototype's IDLE keyframes and timings. The others are new, in the same small scale (at most about 2% of the picture or 3°, loops of 1–3.5s)… One table in code"). Starting at rest matters because the swap from the still picture to the layers happens under the canvas's 120ms fade at the lock: a subject posed off its rest would show a double image for that moment.
- considered: sway starting at −1.8° as the prototype's page does (a visible snap of the bat's or snake's subject at the lock); generating sine keyframes in code for float's tilt (more machinery for a 1.5° tilt); the pivot in the picture's own frame rather than the TV box's (the picture is cut about 1.4px top and bottom by the cover framing, so 88% of the box is 87% of the picture: "about").
- reversible: yes (the table).

## 2026-10-24 — Running it: Web Animations only, found again with getAnimations; cancelled and restarted, not paused (B-221)
- chose: the life is three kinds of `Element.animate` (the subject's idle on `.tv-actor`, the glow on `.tv-plate`, one per speck), with `iterations: Infinity`; no GSAP, no requestAnimationFrame, no timers. picture.js keeps no animation handle: it cancels its animations with `.tv-life.getAnimations({ subtree: true })` and empties `.tv-motes`. They are cancelled when the word changes, when the play screen is left (the round end, the back arrow), when the page is hidden, and are not started under `prefers-reduced-motion` (the layers then show still). Hidden: main.js adds `document.addEventListener('visibilitychange', () => renderPicture(state))`, and renderPicture runs the life only while `!document.hidden`; shown again, it starts afresh (new specks), since a restart at rest is invisible after the page was away. Element.animate, `iterationStart`, `getAnimations({ subtree: true })` and `decode()` are within the browser floor (Chrome 84+ / Safari 14+ at most).
- because: the acceptance ("transforms and opacity, plus the plate's brightness for the glow; no per-frame script; all of it stops on a new word, on leaving the play screen and while the page is hidden"). Without a handle, CLAUDE.md's module-globals rule needs no new exception: picture.js keeps only its render cache (`shown`) and its DOM cache (`dom`). Checked in headless Chromium with a DevTools trace of 3s of a running life (GSAP's ticker asleep so only the life runs): no Paint and no Layout, 14 style updates (the specks' delays ending), so it all runs on the compositor.
- considered: GSAP tweens (GSAP ticks in script every frame); CSS @keyframes per move in game.css (the table would leave the JS and its test); pausing on hidden (needs the handles kept, for nothing the kid would see).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-24 — The win table: the prototype's cheer, bungee and drive as they are; five new moves; an unknown win pops (B-222)
- chose: one table, `WIN` (`winOf`), each move played once. `cheer` (1900ms), `bungee` (1400ms) and `drive` (1500ms, its blur and its four exhaust puffs, `EXHAUST`, the prototype's puff() calls: 80 and 160ms, then 72% and 76% of the move, 1080 and 1140ms) are tv-motion.html's `WIN` keyframes with its timing (`easing: 'ease-in-out'` over the whole move); a test evaluates the prototype's own `WIN` block and compares. The new ones, per-keyframe easings, every one ending where it started: `hop-spin` (1600ms) a crouch, a hop to −30% with one full turn (0 → 360°, done at 62%, before the landing at 66%), a squash 1.16/0.84 on landing, a rebound; `wobble-flop` (1800ms) wobbles −8°/+9°/−5°, topples to −84° from its base, bounces to −74°, lies at −82° about 200ms, springs up past upright to +14°, settles; `splash` (1700ms) dips 7% squashed, leaps to −34% with a 5% sideways arch, tilting −14° rising and +16° falling, splashes in squashed 1.12/0.88, with drops; `pop` (1200ms) a squash 1.12/0.86, a pop to 1.25, bounces 0.92 → 1.09 → 0.97 → 1.02, settles; `zoom` (1500ms) a crouch, shoots up to −110% stretched 0.85/1.3 with a 3px blur, holds there to 38% (the screen empty about 300–350ms; at first −150% with a longer hold left it empty about 560ms, too long for "a beat"), drops back in from above (the blur clearing as it falls), lands squashed, bounces. A % in translate is of the picture. `hop-spin` alone uses the separate `translate`, `rotate` and `scale` properties (each with its own keyframes, so the turn can end before the landing and keep its own easing; Chrome 104+, Safari 14.1+, inside the floor) and its own pivot, the picture's middle (`pivot: 'center'`), so the turn is a somersault, not a cartwheel round its feet; each of its squashes is shifted down by what it lifts a subject standing on the bottom pivot ((1 − scaleY) × 38%), so feet stay on the ground. A `win` the table does not have is `pop` (`lifeOf`), so every correct word has a move; every `win` in today's data is in the table (a test reads the data). Only `drive` and `zoom` blur, and only on their fast part (a test: zoom's blur exactly where it is off the top).
- because: the acceptance (the prototype's three; the five new ones "big and silly, 1.2–1.9s, each ending exactly where it started"; "a win move may also blur its subject for its fast part"). Judged on contact sheets of the TV every 100ms for cat, giraffe, taxi, dog, panda, fish, hen, helicopter and duck (`win-phases.png` keeps one).
- considered: hop-spin in one `transform` list (the turn then follows the hop's easings, still turning as it lands); hop-spin turning round the word's pivot (the dog's feet: it swung below the screen); an unknown win doing nothing (a correct word with only the flash).
- reversible: yes (the table).

## 2026-10-24 — Where the win runs: the subject's img, its idle paused under it; a new .tv-fx layer; the whole picture's pop when no layers show (B-222)
- chose: the move runs on `img.tv-subject` (inside `.tv-actor`, the same box), from the word's pivot (or the move's own), while the actor's idle is paused (the prototype's `idleAnim.pause()`), found with `getAnimations()` and played on when the move's `finished` resolves (a cancelled move rejects it, so nothing is played on after a new word). The paused idle pose and the move compose, so neither the start nor the end jumps (sway's ±1.8° and spin's ±10° would show a snap if the move replaced the idle's transform on one element). The puffs and the drops go in a new `<div class="tv-fx">` after `.tv-actor` in `.tv-life` (over the subject, inside the TV's mask, no taps), each placed in % of the picture and sized from its width, removed when its flight ends. When the layers are not showing at the check (a word without `motion`, today only `duck`; or a word whose layers have not decoded or whose picture has not locked yet), the whole still picture (`#play-image`) does `pop` from its centre, as the acceptance says for a word without `motion`; the flash and the sparks come as always. game.css `?v=34`.

## 2026-10-27 — The quiet gap: from when a clip of his voice last ended or was cut, and while one plays; on performance.now (B-226)
- chose: audio.js notes when his voice last went quiet (`hushedAt`, performance.now() ms; -Infinity before he ever spoke) each time a clip of the voice channel leaves `live`, whether it played to its end or was cut (a speaker tap, leaving the word, the mute, an always moment cutting him). `quietFor()` is the seconds since, or 0 while a clip of his plays or is due, so a sometimes or rare line due while he talks is dropped too, and only an always moment ever cuts him. It is measured once per state change, before any of its moments speak (renderSound's speak), and for each peek whisper (peekLine). The always moments and the word itself are never dropped by it. `hushedAt` is an audio engine handle like `live` (CLAUDE.md's engine-handles bullet names it): no other module reads it, no game logic is decided from it.
- because: the acceptance's "after any line of his ends, a sometimes or rare line is dropped for 8 s": a line cut short also ended, and a line cutting the one he is saying is the opposite of a quiet gap.
- considered: only lines that played to their end (a kid who skips ahead would hear him again at once, and a sometimes line could still cut him); the audio clock (`ctx.currentTime`: stops while the context is suspended, and `chain` already uses it for scheduling, which the tests pin).
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-28 — The iPhone's silent switch: the audio session at the first gesture, a looping silent WAV made in code in an `<audio>` element, paused while muted (B-227)
- chose: audio.js sets `navigator.audioSession.type = 'playback'` (where the browser has it: Safari 17+) at the first gesture that unlocks the sound, in `unlock`, no longer at `init`. There it also makes `keepAlive`: `new Audio()` of a blob URL of `silentWav()` (the bytes of a WAV of 1 s of zeros, 16-bit mono at 8000 Hz, 16044 bytes, as the reference's `silentWav`), `loop = true`. `keepAwake` plays it, unless muted or already playing, at every gesture (a play the browser refuses is tried again on the next). renderSound pauses it when the sound is muted (`silence`) and plays it again on unmute, which is the mute toggle's own tap. `keepAlive` is an audio engine handle, named in CLAUDE.md's engine-handles bullet (else, by CLAUDE.md, a module global is state). With the page playing media, iOS lets the Web Audio sound (his voice, the effects, the hiss, the chime) through the silent switch. Drops no browser: Blob URLs and looping `<audio>` are in the floor, the session is feature-detected.
- because: the acceptance ("At the first gesture that unlocks sound, audio.js sets `navigator.audioSession.type = 'playback'` where the browser has it, and starts a looping silent clip made in code (a short WAV of zeros as a blob URL, in an `<audio>` element) so the page counts as playing media; the mute pauses it, unmute resumes it. Recorded in DECISIONS").
- trade-offs, for the human: while the game is open with sound on, iOS treats it as playing media, so it may show in the lock screen's Now Playing and it stops music from other apps (as a game with sound does); Android Chrome shows no media notification for a 1 s clip. Checked in headless Chromium (the element plays, not paused, its source a blob); not on a real iPhone in this run.
- reversible: yes.

## 2026-10-28 — What is fetched when: nothing of his voice or the sounds before the page is idle; then the manifest and one random clip a pool; one more after each play (B-229)
- chose: audio.js `init` only adds the gesture listeners and `afterIdle(open)`: the page's `load` event, then `requestIdleCallback` (`IDLE.timeout` 1 s at most), or `IDLE.wait` 250 ms after `load` where there is none (Safari). Then the manifest, once, and `firstFetches`: one random clip of each pool the moments use and of each prototype pool (`FILES`), in this order: the pools of a `waitReady` moment (the greeting, which comes with the first tap and waits for its clip), the prototype pools (the swipe on every block, the tada, his fallbacks), his other pools in the moment table's order; a file two pools name (`hmm no`) once. Each time a clip of a pool starts playing (a voice line through `chain`, a pooled clip through `startPool`; a clip cut later still counts), that pool asks for one more it has not asked for (`newClip`, random); a download that fails asks for another of its pool, so one bad file does not leave a pool silent. The prototype pools' clips move from the per-kind `bytes`/`buffers` arrays to one record per URL (`soundClips`), the same record his new voice's clips use; `lineClips` records keep their pool.
- because: BACKLOG B-229 ("Nothing of the robot's voice or the game sounds is fetched before the start screen is shown and idle (after `load`, in `requestIdleCallback` or a short timeout). Then, per pool his moments use, only one clip is fetched and decoded at first; ... after one of its clips plays, one more not yet fetched ... The prototype sound pools the same").
- considered: the first clip of each pool in list order (a returning device would find it in its HTTP cache, but every session would open with the same greeting and the same first lines; random keeps the variety the pools exist for); starting the downloads at the first gesture when it comes before the idle (the acceptance says idle; on a slow network the first visit's greeting is then lost, logged below); growing a pool when its clip ends rather than starts (the same, later).
- reversible: yes (audio.js).

## 2026-10-28 — One download queue: a word's own clips first, then the pools' first clips, then their next; two at a time; the pools wait while a round starts or a picture tunes in (B-229)
- chose: every clip download waits its turn in one queue (`net`, `RANK`: a word's own 0, a pool's first 1, a pool's next 2; `nextDownload`: lowest rank, then the order asked for), `FETCH_AT_ONCE.free` (2) at a time. While `holdsPools(state)` (a round starting, `startingRound`, or a word on the play screen whose picture has not locked, `tuned`) no pool clip starts and a word's own go one at a time (`FETCH_AT_ONCE.held`); as a round starts the pools' clips on their way stop (`toStop`, an AbortController each) and wait their turn again. A word's own clips are asked for as the word starts, in the order they are needed (`wordFetches`: its Hebrew guess, said at the TV's lock; its clip, at its correct check; its gags; its letters' names); a new word lets the last one's go, and those not started leave the queue. The subscribers' order already requests the word's picture (card.js), its layers and win drawings (picture.js) before audio.js asks for its clips.
- because: B-229 "Priority: on a new word its picture, its life layers and its win drawings are requested before any voice clip of that moment; the next word's picture, layers and win drawings are prefetched before more pool clips", and its lock target (under 3 s from the stage tap). On main every clip is asked for at once and the browser queues the round's data and its picture behind them (a 33 s lock when the kid taps early, measured); two at a time keeps the network's latency hidden (32 first clips, about 2.4 MB) without crowding the picture.
- considered: one download at a time (the first clips take about 22 s instead of 13 s at 1.5 Mbps); holding a word's own clips until the lock (its Hebrew guess would then miss the lock it is said at); stopping the pools' downloads at every new word (only the round's first word has no picture fetched ahead; stopping more would throw away bytes five times a round; the cost, seen in a throttled play-through: while the first clips are still coming in, a later word's Hebrew guess can share the network with a pool's clip and miss its lock, and is then skipped as any clip not ready); `fetch(..., { priority: 'low' })` hints (Chrome only, and the queue already orders them).
- reversible: yes (audio.js).

## 2026-10-28 — Measurements, main vs this branch: the lock target met, the 1 MB and 3 s start targets not (B-229)
- method: Chromium over the DevTools protocol, `Network.emulateNetworkConditions` 1.5 Mbps down and 300 ms latency, a fresh profile each run (cold cache), 360x740 at 2x with touch; the local dev server for both (main from a `git worktree` of `main` in /tmp, removed after); a quick kid's flow (picture and go, or the player's card; Animals; station 1). - new device: bytes before the start screen is idle 9.66 MB (173 requests, 6.79 MB of it his voice and the sounds) -> 1.80 MB (81, none of it sound); the start screen drawn 11.4 s -> 7.1 s and fully shown 52.8 s -> 11.0 s; stage tap to the first word's lock 2.66 s (main tapped at about 56 s, after every clip was in) -> 2.36 s; tapping as soon as the start screen is drawn, 33.6 s on main (the round's data and picture behind 6 MB of clips) -> 2.1 s.
- returning player: 9.66 MB -> 1.26 MB before idle; shown 52.8 s -> 7.9 s; lock 2.58 s -> 2.38 s.
